0\to N_{i}\to G_{i,j_{0}}\to H_{j_{0}}\to 0
which pulls back to
0\to N_{i}\to G_{i}\to H_{j_{0}}\to 0
. Taking
G_{i,j}
to be the pullback of
G_{i,j_{0}}
along
H_{j}\to H_{j_{0}}
for
j\geq j_{0}
, we get an exact sequence :

0\to N_{i}\to G_{i,j}\to H_{j}\to 0

in
\mathrm{Ab}_{S}
, where
G_{i}=\varprojlim_{j\geq j_{0}}G_{i,j}
. Clearly the sequence
0\to(N_{i})_{\mathrm{top}}\to(G_{i,j})_{\mathrm{top}}\to(H_{j})_{\mathrm{top}}\to 0
is exact. Taking the cofiltered limit in
j\geq j_{0}
, because the system of kernels
\{(N_{i})_{\mathrm{top}}\}_{j\geq j_{0}}
is constant, one can adapt the proof of
[
34
]
(Lem. 0598) to see the map
(G_{i})_{\mathrm{top}}\to H_{\mathrm{top}}
is surjective for all
i
.

To deduce
G_{\mathrm{top}}\to H_{\mathrm{top}}
is surjective, consider
x\in H_{\mathrm{top}}
and
Z\subseteq G_{\mathrm{top}}
its inverse image. We want to see
Z
is nonempty. Consider also
Z_{i}
the inverse image of
x
in
(G_{i})_{\mathrm{top}}
, for each
i
: these are nonempty by surjectivity of
(G_{i})_{\mathrm{top}}\to H_{\mathrm{top}}
, and we have
Z=\varprojlim_{i}Z_{i}
because limits commute with fiber products. The spaces
H_{\mathrm{top}}
and
(G_{i})_{\mathrm{top}}
are compact Hausdorff and the map
(G_{i})_{\mathrm{top}}\to H_{\mathrm{top}}
is continuous, so
Z_{i}
is compact Hausdorff as a closed subset of
(G_{i})_{\mathrm{top}}
. By Tychonoff’s theorem, as a cofiltered limit of nonempty compact Hausdorff spaces,
Z
is (compact Hausdorff and) nonempty. This concludes.
∎

Proposition 48
.

For
S
a set of primes, the full subcategory
\mathcal{W}_{S}\subseteq\mathrm{IPAb}_{S}
is closed under extensions.

Proof of Lemma
48
.

Consider an exact sequence
0\to H\to E\to G\to 0
in
\mathrm{IPAb}_{S}
such that
H,G\in\mathcal{W}_{S}
.

Case
H
and
G
ind-finite.
Write
H=\varinjlim_{m}H_{m}
and
G=\varinjlim_{n}G_{n}
with
H_{m},G_{n}\in\mathrm{Ab}_{S}
. Let
E_{n}
be the pullback of
E
along
G_{n}\to G
, so we have a short exact sequence
0\to H\to E_{n}\to G_{n}\to 0
with
E=\varinjlim_{n}E_{n}
. By
[
36
]
(Prop. 2.2.2) we have :

\Ext^{1}_{\mathrm{IPAb}_{S}}(G_{n},H)=\Ext^{1}_{\mathrm{IAb}_{S}}(G_{n},H)=\varinjlim_{m}\Ext^{1}_{Ab_{S}}(G_{n},H_{m})

so for some
m_{0}
there exists an exact sequence
0\to H_{m_{0}}\to E_{n,m_{0}}\to G_{n}\to 0
which pushes out to
0\to H\to E_{n}\to G_{n}\to 0
. For
m\geq m_{0}
let
E_{n,m}
be the pushout of
E_{n,m_{0}}
along
H_{m_{0}}\to H_{m}
; then we have exact sequences :

0\to H_{m}\to E_{n,m}\to G_{n}\to 0

so
E_{n,m}\in\mathrm{Ab}_{S}
, and we have
E=\varinjlim_{n}\varinjlim_{m\geq m_{0}}E_{n,m}
. Choosing
\{H_{m}\}_{m}
and
\{G_{n}\}_{n}
to be
\mathbb{N}
-indexed systems, we have an
\mathbb{N}
-indexed colimit
E=\varinjlim_{m\geq m_{0}}E_{m,m}
hence
E\in\mathcal{W}_{S}
.

Case
H
and
G
pro-finite.
The proof is dual to the previous case.

General case.
Consider exact sequences
0\to H^{\prime}\to H\to H^{\prime\prime}\to 0
and
0\to G^{\prime}\to G\to G^{\prime\prime}\to 0
where
H^{\prime},G^{\prime}
are
\mathbb{N}
-indexed objects of
\mathrm{PAb}_{S}
and
H^{\prime\prime},G^{\prime\prime}
are
\mathbb{N}
-indexed objects of
\mathrm{IAb}_{S}
. Write
H^{\prime\prime}=\varinjlim_{m}H^{\prime\prime}_{m}
with
H_{m}^{\prime\prime}\in\mathrm{Ab}_{S}
and let
H_{m}\subseteq H
be the pullback of
H^{\prime\prime}_{m}
. The exact sequence
0\to H^{\prime}\to H_{m}\to H^{\prime\prime}_{m}\to 0
and the pro-finite case show
H_{m}\in\mathcal{W}_{S}
is pro-finite, and we have
H=\varinjlim_{m}H_{m}
.

By
[
36
]
(Prop. 2.2.2) , the sequence
0\to H\to E^{\prime}\to G^{\prime}\to 0
is the pushout of some exact sequence
0\to H_{m}\to E_{m}^{\prime}\to G^{\prime}\to 0
. By the pro-finite case,
E_{m}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{W}_{S}
is pro-finite. By definition of
H_{m}
we have :

H/H_{m}=H^{\prime\prime}/H^{\prime\prime}_{m}=\varinjlim_{n\geq m}H_{n}^{\prime\prime}/H_{m}^{\prime\prime}

so
H/H_{m}\in\mathcal{W}_{S}
is ind-finite. The exact sequence
0\to H/H_{m}\to E/E^{\prime}_{m}\to G^{\prime\prime}\to 0
shows
E/E_{m}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{W}_{S}
is ind-finite by the ind-finite case. This concludes.
∎

Proposition 49
.

Let
S
be a fixed set of primes.

1.

The functor
(-)_{\mathrm{cond}}
induces a fully faithful functor
D^{b}(\mathrm{LCAb})\to\Sh(*_{\mathrm{proet}})
. In particular,
(-)_{\mathrm{cond}}
makes
\mathrm{LCAb}
into a full subcategory of
\Sh(*_{\mathrm{proet}})
closed under extensions.

2.

The Yoneda functor induces fully faithful exact functors of abelian and triangulated categories :

Y:\mathrm{IPAb}_{S}\to\Sh(*_{\mathrm{proet}}),\qquad RY:D^{b}(\mathrm{IPAb}_{S})\to D(*_{\mathrm{proet}})

identifying
\mathrm{IPAb}_{S}
as a full abelian subcategory of
\Sh(*_{\mathrm{proet}})
closed under extensions and
D^{b}(\mathrm{IPAb}_{S})
as a full triangulated subcategory of
D(*_{\mathrm{proet}})
.

3.

For
G\in\mathcal{W}_{S}
,
G_{\mathrm{top}}
is locally compact Hausdorff and
(G_{\mathrm{top}})_{\mathrm{cond}}=Y(G)
in
\Sh(*_{\mathrm{proet}})
.

4.

The functor
(-)_{\mathrm{top}}:\mathcal{W}_{S}\to\mathrm{LCAb}
is a fully faithful exact functor of exact categories which identifies
\mathcal{W}_{S}
as a full subcategory closed under extensions.

In contrast, the topological realization of a general
G\in\mathrm{IPAb}_{\mathrm{fin}}
may not be locally compact Hausdorff. For example, the topological realization of
(\prod_{i}F_{i})/(\bigoplus_{i}F_{i})
for any infinite family of nontrivial finite groups
F_{i}
, is not Hausdorff. This example also shows
\mathcal{W}_{S}
is not stable under quotients. The bounded derived category
D^{b}(\mathrm{LCAb})
of the nonabelian category
\mathrm{LCAb}
is defined in
[
15
]
; for
G,H\in\mathrm{LCAb}
and
q\in\geq 0
, the group
\Hom_{D^{b}(\mathrm{LCAb})}(G,H[q])
coincides with the group of Yoneda extensions
\Ext^{q}_{\mathrm{LCAb}}(G,H)
(where one replaces the notion of exact sequences with that of strict exact sequences).

Proof of Proposition
49
.

1.
This is
[
5
]
(Cor. 4.9). See
[
15
]
for more on category
D^{b}(\mathrm{LCAb})
.

2.

Exactness of
Y
.
Because filtered colimits are exact, it suffices to prove that the restriction
Y:\mathrm{PAb}_{S}\to\Sh(*_{\mathrm{proet}})
is exact. Left-exactness is generally true of the Yoneda functor. For right-exactness, if
G\in\mathrm{PAb}_{S}
, then
Y(G)\in\Sh(*_{\mathrm{proet}})
is precisely the sheaf representable by the pro-finite group
G_{\mathrm{top}}
, so by Lemma
47
, a surjection
G\to H
in
\mathrm{PAb}_{S}
gives a continuous surjection
Y(G)\to Y(H)
.

Full faithfulness of
RY
.
By
[
17
]
(Prop. 15.3.2), the functor
RY:D^{b}(\mathrm{IPAb}_{S})\to\Sh(*_{\mathrm{proet}})
is well defined. By
[
36
]
(Prop. 2.2.3) applied to the functor
\Hom_{\Sh(*_{\mathrm{proet}})}(Y(-),Y(-))
, for
A=\varinjlim_{i}\varprojlim_{j}A_{ij}
and
B=\varinjlim_{k}\varprojlim_{l}B_{kl}
with
A_{ij},B_{kl}\in\mathrm{Ab}_{S}
, we get a sequence of morphisms and isomorphisms :

\displaystyle R\varprojlim_{i}\varinjlim_{k}R\varprojlim_{l}\varinjlim_{j}R\Hom_{*_{\mathrm{proet}}}(Y(A_{ij}),Y(B_{kl}))

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle R\varprojlim_{i}\varinjlim_{k}R\varprojlim_{l}R\Hom_{*_{\mathrm{proet}}}(\varprojlim_{j}Y(A_{ij}),Y(B_{kl}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle R\varprojlim_{i}\varinjlim_{k}R\Hom_{*_{\mathrm{proet}}}(\varprojlim_{j}Y(A_{ij}),R\varprojlim_{l}Y(B_{kl}))

\displaystyle\to

\displaystyle R\varprojlim_{i}R\Hom_{*_{\mathrm{proet}}}(\varprojlim_{j}Y(A_{ij}),\varinjlim_{k}R\varprojlim_{l}Y(B_{kl}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle R\Hom_{*_{\mathrm{proet}}}(\varinjlim_{i}\varprojlim_{j}Y(A_{ij}),\varinjlim_{k}R\varprojlim_{l}Y(B_{kl}))

As seen in the previous point,
R\varprojlim_{l}Y(B_{kl})=\varprojlim_{l}Y(B_{kl})
, so the last term is simply
R\Hom_{*_{\mathrm{proet}}}(Y(A),Y(B))
. On the other hand, we have :

R\Hom_{*_{\mathrm{proet}}}(Y(A_{ij}),Y(B_{kl}))=RY(R\Hom_{\mathrm{Ab}_{S}}(A_{ij},B_{kl}))

for
A_{ij},B_{kl}\in\mathrm{Ab}_{S}
as a very particular case of
(1.)
since
R\Hom_{\mathrm{LCAb}}(A_{ij},B_{kl})
is the complex of discrete groups
R\Hom_{\mathrm{Ab}}(A_{ij},B_{kl})
for the discrete abelian groups
A_{ij}
and
B_{kl}
, by
[
15
]
(Rem. 4.17). Hence the first term in the sequence is
R\Hom_{\mathrm{IPAb}_{S}}(A,B)
.

Hence it remains to show that we have isomorphisms for
A_{ij},B_{kl}\in\mathrm{Ab}_{S}
:

\displaystyle\varinjlim_{k}R\Hom_{*_{\mathrm{proet}}}(\varprojlim_{j}Y(A_{ij}),R\varprojlim_{l}Y(B_{kl}))

\displaystyle\to R\Hom_{*_{\mathrm{proet}}}(\varprojlim_{j}Y(A_{ij}),\varinjlim_{k}R\varprojlim_{l}Y(B_{kl})),

\displaystyle\varinjlim_{j}R\Hom_{*_{\mathrm{proet}}}(Y(A_{ij}),Y(B_{kl}))

\displaystyle\to R\Hom_{*_{\mathrm{proet}}}(\varprojlim_{j}Y(A_{ij}),Y(B_{kl})).

Since
R\varprojlim_{l}Y(B_{kl})=\varprojlim_{l}Y(B_{kl})
by
(1.)
, it is enough to show for
X=\varprojlim_{j}X_{j}
any cofiltered limit in
\mathrm{PAb}_{S}
and
Z=\varinjlim_{i}Z_{i}
any filtered colimit in
\Sh(*_{\mathrm{proet}})
, the equality :

\varinjlim_{i}\varinjlim_{j}R\HOM_{*_{\mathrm{proet}}}(Y(X_{j}),Z_{i})=R\HOM_{*_{\mathrm{proet}}}(Y(X),Z)

For
G
an abelian group, recall
M_{\bullet}(G)\to G
the Deligne-Scholze resolution (
[
38
]
§3.4 or
[
5
]
Th. 4.5) : this is a resolution concentrated in nonnegative (homological) degree, such that
M_{n}(G)=\bigoplus_{i=1}^{r_{n}}\mathbb{Z}[G^{m_{i,n}}]
for some integers
r_{n},m_{i,n}\geq 0
, where
\mathbb{Z}[G^{m}]
means the free abelian group generated by the set
G^{m}
. The resolution
M_{\bullet}(G)\to G
and isomorphisms
M_{n}(G)=\bigoplus_{i=1}^{r_{n}}\mathbb{Z}[G^{m_{i,n}}]
are functorial in
G
: thus for any sheaf
F\in\Sh(*_{\mathrm{proet}})
, we similarly have a resolution
M_{\bullet}(F)\to F
with
M_{n}(F)\to\bigoplus_{i=1}^{r_{n}}\mathbb{Z}[F^{m_{i,n}}]
, where
M_{n}(F)
and
\mathbb{Z}[F^{m}]
mean the sheafifications of the presheaves
U\mapsto M_{n}(F(U))
and
U\mapsto\mathbb{Z}[F(U)]
. We have a functorial spectral sequence and natural identifications on
\mathrm{PAb}_{S}\times\Sh(*_{\mathrm{proet}})
:

\displaystyle E^{pq}_{1}(-,-)=

\displaystyle\Ext^{p}_{*_{\mathrm{proet}}}(M_{q}(Y(-)),-)\Rightarrow\Ext^{p+q}_{*_{\mathrm{proet}}}(M_{\bullet}(Y(-)),-)=\Ext^{p+q}_{*_{\mathrm{proet}}}(Y(-),-),

\displaystyle E^{pq}_{1}(-,-)=

\displaystyle\bigoplus_{i=1}^{r_{q}}\Ext^{p}_{*_{\mathrm{proet}}}(\mathbb{Z}[Y(-)^{m_{i,q}}],-)=\bigoplus_{i=1}^{r_{q}}H^{q}((-)^{m_{i,q}},-)

and the claim reduces to the equalities
\varinjlim_{i}\varinjlim_{j}H^{q}(X_{j}^{m},Z_{i})=H^{q}(X,Z)
of
[
34
]
(Lem. 0739), for
m\geq 0
.

Full faithfulness of
Y
and stability under extensions.
These follow from the full faithfulness of
RY
and exactness of
Y
, which imply
\Hom_{*_{\mathrm{proet}}}(Y(A),Y(B))=\Hom_{\mathrm{IPAb}_{S}}(A,B)
and
\Ext^{1}_{*_{\mathrm{proet}}}(Y(A),Y(B))=\Ext^{1}_{\mathrm{IPAb}_{S}}(A,B)
for
A,B\in\mathrm{IPAb}_{S}
.

3.
Consider an exact sequence
0\to G^{\prime}\to G\to G^{\prime\prime}\to 0
such that
G^{\prime}=\varprojlim_{m}G_{m}^{\prime}
and
G^{\prime\prime}=\varinjlim_{n}G^{\prime\prime}_{n}
with
G^{\prime}_{m},G^{\prime\prime}_{n}\in\mathrm{Ab}_{S}
indexed by
\mathbb{N}
. We will show that the sequence
0\to G^{\prime}_{\mathrm{top}}\to G_{\mathrm{top}}\to G^{\prime\prime}_{\mathrm{top}}\to 0
is topologically exact. Then
G^{\prime}_{\mathrm{top}}
is a pro-finite open subgroup of
G_{\mathrm{top}}
because
G^{\prime\prime}_{\mathrm{top}}
is discrete, so
G_{\mathrm{top}}
is locally pro-finite (hence locally compact Hausdorff).

Define
G_{n}
and
G_{n,m}
by the following commutative diagram with exact rows, cartesian top-right square and cocartesian bottom-left square :

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G^{\prime\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G_{n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G^{\prime\prime}_{n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G^{\prime}_{m}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G_{n,m}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G^{\prime\prime}_{n}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}

where
G_{n}=\varprojlim_{m}G_{n,m}
and
G=\varinjlim_{n}G_{n}
in
\mathrm{IPAb}_{S}
. By definition the groups
(G^{\prime}_{m})_{\mathrm{top}}
,
(G_{n,m})_{\mathrm{top}}
and
(G^{\prime\prime}_{n})_{\mathrm{top}}
are finite discrete, so the sequences
0\to(G^{\prime}_{m})_{\mathrm{top}}\to(G_{n,m})_{\mathrm{top}}\to(G^{\prime\prime}_{n})_{\mathrm{top}}\to 0
are trivially topologically exact.

Let
N_{m}^{\prime}=\ker(G^{\prime}\to G_{m}^{\prime})
and
N_{n,m}=\ker(G_{n}\to G_{n,m})
. The snake lemma gives an isomorphism
N_{m}^{\prime}=N_{n,m}
. By left exactness of limits, the induced map
u_{n}:G^{\prime}_{\mathrm{top}}\to(G_{n})_{\mathrm{top}}
is injective and we have an identification of subgroups (without topology) :

\ker(G^{\prime}_{\mathrm{top}}\to(G^{\prime}_{m})_{\mathrm{top}})=\varprojlim_{m\leq m^{\prime}}\ker((G^{\prime}_{m^{\prime}})_{\mathrm{top}}\to(G^{\prime}_{m})_{\mathrm{top}})=\ker(G^{\prime}\to G^{\prime}_{m})_{\mathrm{top}}=(N_{m}^{\prime})_{\mathrm{top}}

and similarly
\ker((G_{n})_{\to}(G_{n,m})_{\mathrm{top}})=(N_{n,m})_{\mathrm{top}}
. Thus by definition of the pro-finite topologies on
(G_{n})_{\mathrm{top}}
and
G^{\prime}_{\mathrm{top}}
, the groups
(N_{m}^{\prime})_{\mathrm{top}}=(N_{n,m})_{\mathrm{top}}
for varying
m
form a basis of open neighborhoods of
0
in
G^{\prime}_{\mathrm{top}}
and in
(G_{n})_{\mathrm{top}}
simultaneously. Since
u_{n}
is a group homomorphism, this implies
u_{n}
is open. As an open injection, it is an open immersion, thus
(G_{n})_{\mathrm{top}}/G^{\prime}_{\mathrm{top}}
is discrete, hence coincides with
(G^{\prime\prime}_{n})_{\mathrm{top}}
as a topological group. Thus the sequences
0\to G^{\prime}_{\mathrm{top}}\to(G_{n})_{\mathrm{top}}\to(G_{n}^{\prime\prime})_{\mathrm{top}}\to 0
are topologically exact.

By exactness of filtered colimits of abelian groups, the sequence
0\to G^{\prime}_{\mathrm{top}}\to G_{\mathrm{top}}\to G^{\prime\prime}_{\mathrm{top}}\to 0
is exact in
\mathrm{Ab}
(without topologies). Let
u:G^{\prime}_{\mathrm{top}}\to G_{\mathrm{top}}
be the corresponding injection. By definition of the colimit topology (using here that
G=\varinjlim_{n}G_{n}
is a countable colimit, see also Remark
46
),
u
is an open immersion if and only if
u_{n}:G^{\prime}_{\mathrm{top}}\to(G_{n})_{\mathrm{top}}
is an open immersion for each
n
, which we have just seen. In particular
G_{\mathrm{top}}/G^{\prime}_{\mathrm{top}}
is discrete. The group
G^{\prime\prime}_{\mathrm{top}}
is discrete, thus the isomorphism of abelian groups
G_{\mathrm{top}}/G^{\prime}_{\mathrm{top}}\cong G^{\prime\prime}_{\mathrm{top}}
is a homeomorphism, and this sequence is topologically exact. This concludes.

Now we show
(G_{\mathrm{top}})_{\mathrm{cond}}=Y(G)
. Consider
G=\varinjlim_{n}G_{n}
as above. For
X
a pro-finite set, we have by definition :

(G_{\mathrm{top}})_{\mathrm{cond}}(X)=\Hom(X,G_{\mathrm{top}}),\qquad Y(G)(X)=\varinjlim_{n}\Hom(X,(G_{n})_{\mathrm{top}})

where the Hom’s here denote continuous maps. We can assume the transitions in
G^{\prime\prime}_{\mathrm{top}}=\varinjlim_{n}(G_{n}^{\prime\prime})_{\mathrm{top}}
(hence in
G_{\mathrm{top}}=\varinjlim_{n}(G_{n})_{\mathrm{top}}
) are injective, because the
G_{n}^{\prime\prime}
’s are finite. Then the canonical map :

\varinjlim_{n}\Hom(X,(G_{n})_{\mathrm{top}})\to\Hom(X,G_{\mathrm{top}})

is injective. If
f:X\to G_{\mathrm{top}}
is a continuous map then the composite
X\to G_{\mathrm{top}}\to G^{\prime\prime}_{\mathrm{top}}
has compact and discrete, hence finite image, thus factors through one of the subgroups
(G^{\prime\prime}_{n})_{\mathrm{top}}\leq G^{\prime\prime}_{\mathrm{top}}
. Thus
f
factors through some
(G_{n})_{\mathrm{top}}
. Thus the canonical map above is bijective, and
(G_{\mathrm{top}})_{\mathrm{cond}}(X)=Y(G)(X)
.

4.
The fully faithful exactness follows from
(1.)
,
(2.)
and
(3.)
. Stability under extensions similarly reduces to
\mathcal{W}_{S}
being closed under extensions in
\mathrm{IPAb}_{S}
, which is Lemma
48
.
∎

3.2
Duality for ind-pro-finite groups

Definition 50
.

Let
S
be a fixed set of primes.

—

We write
D^{b}_{\mathcal{W}_{S}}(*_{\mathrm{proet}})\subseteq D^{b}(*_{\mathrm{proet}})
and
D^{b}_{\mathcal{W}_{S}}(\mathrm{IPAb}_{S})\subseteq D^{b}(\mathrm{IPAb}_{S})
the full sucategories of bounded objects with cohomology in (or representable by objects of)
\mathcal{W}_{S}
.

—

We write
\langle\mathcal{W}_{S}\rangle_{*_{\mathrm{proet}}}\subseteq D(*_{\mathrm{proet}})
and
\langle\mathcal{W}_{S}\rangle_{\mathrm{IPAb}_{S}}\subseteq D^{b}(\mathrm{IPAb}_{S})
the smallest full triangulated sucategories which contains (objects representably by) objects of
\mathcal{W}_{S}
in degree
0
.

Proposition 51
.

Let
S
be a fixed set of primes.

1.

The functor
\Gamma(*_{\mathrm{proet}},-):\Sh(*_{\mathrm{proet}})\to\mathrm{Ab}
commutes with all limits and colimits. For
G\in\mathrm{TopAb}
,
\Gamma(*,G_{\mathrm{cond}})
is the abelian group underlying
G
(without topology).

2.

The derived Yoneda functor
RY:D^{b}(\mathrm{IPAb}_{S})\to D(*_{\mathrm{proet}})
induces exact equivalences :

D^{b}_{\mathcal{W}_{S}}(\mathrm{IPAb}_{S})\xrightarrow{\sim}D^{b}_{\mathcal{W}_{S}}(*_{\mathrm{proet}}),\qquad\langle\mathcal{W}_{S}\rangle_{\mathrm{IPAb}_{S}}\xrightarrow{\sim}\langle\mathcal{W}_{S}\rangle_{*_{\mathrm{proet}}}.

3.

For objects
A=\varinjlim_{i}\varprojlim_{j}A_{ij}
and
B=\varinjlim_{k}\varprojlim_{l}B_{kl}
of
\mathrm{IPAb}_{S}
with
A_{ij},B_{kl}\in\mathrm{Ab}_{S}
, we have :

R\HOM_{*_{\mathrm{proet}}}(A,B)=R\varprojlim_{i}\varinjlim_{k}R\varprojlim_{j}\varinjlim_{l}R\HOM_{*_{\mathrm{proet}}}(A_{ij},B_{kl})

where
R\HOM_{*_{\mathrm{proet}}}(A_{ij},B_{kl})
is representable by
R\Hom_{\mathrm{Ab}}(A_{ij},B_{kl})\in D^{b}(\mathrm{Ab}_{S})\subseteq D^{b}(\mathrm{IPAb}_{S})
.

4.

Let
G\in D^{b}_{\mathcal{W}_{S}}(*_{\mathrm{proet}})
and
H=R\HOM_{*_{\mathrm{proet}}}(G,\mathbb{Q}_{p}/\mathbb{Z}_{p})
. Then
H\in D^{b}_{\mathcal{W}_{S}}(*_{\mathrm{proet}})
and for
q\in\mathbb{Z}
:

H^{q}(H)=\HOM_{*_{\mathrm{proet}}}(H^{-q}(G),\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})

making
H^{q}(H)_{\mathrm{top}}
and
H^{-q}(G)_{\mathrm{top}}
perfect Pontryagin dual locally compact Hausdorff groups.

Proof of Proposition
51
.

1.
This is
[
5
]
(proof of Th. 2.2).

2.
These follow from Proposition
49
.2.

3.
This reduces to the analogous formula for
R\Hom_{*_{\mathrm{proet}}/U}((-)|_{U},(-)|_{U})
, for each
U\in*_{\mathrm{proet}}
, for which the proof is identical to the proof of full faithfulness of
RY
in Proposition
49
.2, and the identification of
R\HOM_{\mathrm{LCAb}}(A,B)
with the complex of discrete groups
R\Hom_{\mathrm{Ab}}(A,B)
for
A,B\in\mathrm{Ab}_{S}
.

4.
If
G=\varprojlim_{i}G_{i}\in\mathrm{PAb}_{S}
with
G_{i}\in\mathrm{Ab}_{S}
, then by
(3.)
and exactness of
\Hom_{\mathrm{Ab}}(-,\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})
we have :

R\HOM_{*_{\mathrm{proet}}}(Y(G),\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})=\varinjlim_{i}R\HOM_{*_{\mathrm{proet}}}(Y(G_{i}),\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})=\varinjlim_{i}Y(\HOM_{\mathrm{Ab}}(G_{i},\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z}))=Y(H)

where
H=\varinjlim_{i}\Hom_{\mathrm{Ab}_{S}}(G_{i},\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})
belongs to
\mathrm{IAb}_{S}
, and clearly
H_{\mathrm{top}}
is the Pontryagin dual of
G_{\mathrm{top}}
. We conclude similarly in the case
G\in\mathrm{IAb}_{S}
(using the fact that
R\varprojlim_{i}Y(H_{i})=\varprojlim_{i}Y(H_{i})
in
D(*_{\mathrm{proet}})
for
H_{i}\in\mathrm{Ab}_{S}
). The general case
G\in\mathcal{W}_{S}
follows immediately.

Now for
G\in D^{b}_{\mathcal{W}_{S}}(*_{\mathrm{proet}})
, letting
H=R\HOM_{*_{\mathrm{proet}}}(G,\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})
, the spectral sequence :

E^{ij}_{2}=\EXT^{i}_{*_{\mathrm{proet}}}(H^{-j}(G),\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})\Rightarrow H^{i+j}(H)

degenerates at the first page (
E^{ij}_{2}=0
for
i\neq 0
by the case
G\in\mathcal{W}_{S}
), giving the desired isomorphism. This proves
H\in D^{b}_{\mathcal{W}_{S}}(*_{\mathrm{proet}})
, and the Pontryagin duality statement follows.
∎

3.3
Cohomology of a finite field

Situation 52
.

Let
k
be a finite field of characteristic
p
. Consider the premorphism
\psi_{k/*}:k^{\mathrm{indrat}}_{\mathrm{proet}}\to*_{\mathrm{proet}}
given on the underlying categories by :

\psi_{k/*}^{-1}(S)=\varprojlim_{i}\left(\bigsqcup_{s\in S_{i}}\Spec k\right)\in k^{\mathrm{indrat}}_{\mathrm{proet}}

for
S=\varprojlim S_{i}
a pro-finite set with finite
S_{i}
. We write
\Psi_{k/*}:\Sh(k_{\mathrm{proet}}^{\mathrm{indrat}})\to\Sh(*_{\mathrm{proet}})
its pushforward.

In
[
40
]
, the functor
R\Psi_{k/*}
is simply written
R\Gamma(k,-)
. This notation is justified by the equality
R^{q}\Psi_{k/*}(G)(*)=H^{q}(k_{\mathrm{proet}},G)

for
G\in D(k_{\mathrm{proet}}^{\mathrm{indrat}})
and
q\in\mathbb{Z}
, due to the exactness of
R\Gamma(*_{\mathrm{proet}},-)
. Hence
R^{q}\Psi_{k/*}(G)
can truly be thought of as the group
H^{q}(k_{\mathrm{proet}},G)
enhanced with the structure of a sheaf over
*_{\mathrm{proet}}
,
i.e.
a condensed group. Nevertheless we preferred a notation that does not conflict with the (non-condensed) cohomology groups, and reminds one of the functors
R\Psi_{L/l}
defined in the next part, though in the case of Situation
52
the functor
R\Psi_{k/*}
is unrelated to nearby cycles.

Lemma 53
.

Let
f:X\to Y
be a premorphism of sites. For
G\in\Sh(X)
and
q\in\mathbb{Z}
, the sheaf
R^{q}f_{*}(G)
is the sheafification of the presheaf
U\in Y\mapsto H^{q}(f^{-1}(U),G)
.

Proof of Lemma
53
.

Consider
a:\mathrm{PSh}(Y)\to\Sh(Y)
the sheafification functor and
F:\Sh(X)\to\mathrm{PSh}(Y)
the functor given by
F(G)(U)=G(f^{-1}(U))
for
U\in Y
. By exactness of
\Gamma(U,-)
on
\mathrm{PSh}(Y)
, we have :

\Gamma(U,-)\circ R^{q}F=R^{q}[\Gamma(U,-)\circ F]=H^{q}(f^{-1}(U),-).

Thus
aR^{q}F(G)
is exactly the sheafification of
U\mapsto H^{q}(f^{-1}U,G)
, while on the other hand
aF(G)
is
f_{*}(G)
by definition of the pushforward. The equality
R^{q}(aF)=aR^{q}F
follows from the case
q=0
by exactness of
a
, proving the claim.
∎

Proposition 54
.

Consider
k
and
p
as in Situation
52
.

1.

The functor
R\Psi_{k/*}
sends
D^{b}_{\mathcal{W}_{k}}(k_{\mathrm{proet}}^{\mathrm{indrat}})
to
D^{b}_{\mathcal{W}_{p}}(*_{\mathrm{proet}})
. In particular for
G\in\langle\mathcal{W}_{k}\rangle_{k_{\mathrm{proet}}^{\mathrm{indrat}}}^{H}
and
q\in\mathbb{Z}
,
R^{q}\Psi_{k/*}(G)
is representable by a locally compact Haudorff topological abelian group.

2.

For
G\in D^{b}_{\mathcal{W}_{k}}(k_{\mathrm{proet}}^{\mathrm{indrat}})
, the object
R\Psi_{k/*}(G)\in\mathcal{W}_{p}
is computed as follows.

(a)

If
G\in\Alg_{u}^{\mathrm{RP}}(k)
then for all
q\in\mathbb{Z}
the object
R^{q}\Psi_{k/*}(G)
is representable by the discrete finite group
H^{q}(k_{\mathrm{et}},G)
. In particular
R^{q}\Psi_{k/*}(G)=0
for
q\neq 0,1
.

(b)

If
G\in\mathcal{W}_{k}
is connected then
R\Psi_{k/*}(G)
is concentrated in degree
0
and
R^{0}\Psi_{k/*}(G)\in\mathcal{W}_{p}
. The functor
R^{0}\Psi_{k/*}
sends type 1 and type 2 systems to limits and colimits in
\Sh(*_{\mathrm{proet}})
, respectively.

(c)

If
G\in\mathcal{W}_{k}
then
R^{q}\Psi_{k/*}(G)=0
for
q\neq 0,1
,
R^{1}\Psi_{k/*}(G)=R^{1}\Psi_{k/*}(\pi_{0}(G))
belongs to
\mathrm{Ab}_{p}
, and
R^{0}\Psi_{k/*}(G)
belongs to
\mathcal{W}_{p}
.

(d)

For general
G\in D^{b}_{\mathcal{W}_{k}}(k_{\mathrm{proet}}^{\mathrm{indrat}})
and
q\in\mathbb{Z}
, there is an exact sequence :

0\to R^{1}\Psi_{k/*}(\pi_{0}(H^{q-1}(G)))\to R^{q}\Psi_{k/*}(G)\to R^{0}\Psi_{k/*}(H^{q}(G))\to 0

whose topological realization is topologically exact.

3.

There is a trace map
\mathrm{tr}:R\Psi_{k/*}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}/\mathbb{Z}_{p})\to\mathbb{Q}_{p}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}[-1]
.

4.

For
G\in D^{b}_{\mathcal{W}_{k}}(k_{\mathrm{proet}}^{\mathrm{indrat}})
and
G^{\vee}=R\HOM_{k_{\mathrm{proet}}^{\mathrm{indrat}}}(G,\mathbb{Q}_{p}/\mathbb{Z}_{p})
, we have a perfect pairing in
D(*_{\mathrm{proet}})
:

R\Psi_{k/*}(G)\otimes^{L}R\Psi_{k/*}(G^{\vee})\to R\Psi_{k/*}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}/\mathbb{Z}_{p})\xrightarrow{\mathrm{tr}}\mathbb{Q}_{p}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}[-1]

which induces perfect Pontryagin duality of locally compact Hausdorff abelian groups for
q\in\mathbb{Z}
:

R^{q}\Psi_{k/*}(G)\otimes R^{1-q}\Psi_{k/*}(G^{\vee})\to R^{1}\Psi_{k/*}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}/\mathbb{Z}_{p})\to\mathbb{Q}_{p}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}.

Proof of Proposition
54
.

1.
This will follow from the computations of
(2.)
and Proposition
49
.3.

2.a.
Consider
G=G_{0}^{\mathrm{RP}}\in\Alg_{u}^{\mathrm{RP}}(k)
with
G_{0}\in\Alg_{u}(k)
,
S
a finite set and
k^{\prime}=\psi^{-1}_{k/*}(S)
. Clearly
H^{0}(k^{\prime}_{\mathrm{et}},G)=G_{0}(k)^{S}
is finite. We have
H^{1}(k^{\prime}_{\mathrm{et}},G)=H^{1}(k_{\mathrm{et}},\pi_{0}(G^{0}))^{S}
by
[
2
]
(Prop. 8.9) and
[
8
]
(Cor. 7.10), and this group is finite by
[
32
]
(Prop. XIII.1). For
q\geq 2
,
H^{q}(k^{\prime}_{\mathrm{et}},G)=H^{q}(k_{\mathrm{et}},G)^{S}
is trivial because
G
is torsion. Hence
H^{q}(k^{\prime}_{\mathrm{et}},G)
is finite for
q\in\mathbb{Z}
. The claim follows from Proposition
41
.4 and Lemma
53
.

2.b.
By
[
36
]
(Prop. 2.2.4) for
G=\varinjlim_{i}\varprojlim_{j}G_{ij}\in\mathrm{IPAlg}_{u}^{\mathrm{RP}}(k)
with
G_{ij}\in\Alg^{\mathrm{RP}}_{u}(k)
we have :

R\Psi_{k/*}(G)=\varinjlim_{i}R\varprojlim_{j}R\Psi_{k/*}(G_{ij}).

If
G=\varinjlim_{n}G_{n}
is type 2 then for all
q\in\mathbb{Z}
we have
R^{q}\Psi_{k/*}(G)=\varinjlim_{n}R^{q}\Psi_{k/*}(G_{n})
, and the cancellation follows from
(2.a)
. If
G=\varprojlim_{n}G_{n}
is type 1 then we have a spectral sequence :

E^{ij}_{2}=R^{i}\varprojlim_{i}R^{j}\Psi_{k/*}(G_{i})\Rightarrow R^{i+j}\Psi_{k/*}(G).

By
(2.a)
we have
R^{j}\Psi_{k/*}(G_{i})=0
if
j\geq 1
. In particular the transitions
R^{0}\Psi_{k/*}(G_{i+1})\to R^{0}\Psi_{k/*}(G_{i})
are surjective, so by
[
34
]
(Lem. 0CQA and (1) of Lem. 07KW),
E^{i0}_{2}=0
for
i\geq 1
. This implies
R^{q}\Psi_{k/*}(G)=0
for
q\geq 1
, and
R^{0}\Psi_{k/*}(G)=\varprojlim_{i}R^{0}\Psi_{k/*}(G_{i})
, as desired.

2.c
The long exact sequence for
R\Psi_{k/*}(G)
, combined with the cancellations
R^{q}\Psi_{k/*}(\pi_{0}(G))=0
for
q\geq 2
and
R^{q}\Psi_{k/*}(G^{0})=0
for
q\geq 1
of
(2.a)
and
(2.b)
, give an isomorphism
R^{1}\Psi_{k/*}(G)=R^{1}\Psi_{k/*}(\pi_{0}(G))
and an exact sequence
0\to R^{0}\Psi_{k/*}(G^{0})\to R^{0}\Psi_{k/*}(G)\to R^{0}\Psi_{k/*}(\pi_{0}(G))\to 0
. We conclude by
(2.a)
,
(2.b)
and the stability of
\mathcal{W}_{p}
under extensions in
\Sh(*_{\mathrm{proet}})
(Lemma
48
and Proposition
49
.2).

2.d
We have a spectral sequence :

E^{ij}_{2}=R^{i}\Psi_{k/*}(H^{j}(G))\Rightarrow R^{i+j}\Psi_{k/*}(G)

where
H^{j}(G)\in\mathcal{W}_{k}
for all
j\in\mathbb{Z}
. By
(2.c)
,
E^{ij}_{2}=0
for all
j\geq 2
so this spectral sequence degenerates to short exact sequences
0\to R^{1}\Psi_{k/*}(H^{q-1}(G))\to R^{q}\Psi_{k/*}(G)\to R^{0}\Psi_{k/*}(H^{q}(G))\to 0
where
R^{1}\Psi_{k/*}(H^{q-1}(G))=R^{1}\Psi_{k/*}(\pi_{0}(H^{q-1}(G)))
by
(2.c)
.

3.
The isomorphism
H^{1}(k_{\mathrm{et}},\mathbb{Z}/p^{n})=\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}
for
k
finite is well known, given by identifying the Galois group of
k
with
\hat{\mathbb{Z}}
and
H^{1}(k_{\mathrm{et}},\mathbb{Z}/p^{n})
with the set of continuous,
p^{n}
-torsion characters of this Galois group. The result for
R^{1}\Psi_{k/*}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}/\mathbb{Z}_{p})=\varinjlim_{n}R^{1}\Psi_{k/*}(\mathbb{Z}/p^{n})=\varinjlim H^{1}(k_{\mathrm{et}},\mathbb{Z}/p^{n})
follows. The morphism
R\Psi_{k/*}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}/\mathbb{Z}_{p})\to\mathbb{Q}_{p}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}[-1]
then comes from the fact that
R\Psi_{k/*}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}/\mathbb{Z}_{p})=0
for
q>1
.

4.
See
[
40
]
(Prop. 12.2) and Proposition
51
.4.
∎

3.4
Finite coefficients

Definition 55
.

Consider
X
a scheme and
S_{X}
any of the following sites :

1.

S_{X}=X_{\mathrm{et}}
with no assumption  ;

2.

S_{X}=k_{\mathrm{Et}}
if
X=\Spec k
with
k
a field  ;

3.

S_{X}=k_{\mathrm{RP}},k_{\mathrm{RPS}}
if
X=\Spec k
with
k
a field of characteristic
p>0
such that
[k:k^{p}]<\infty
;

4.

S_{X}=k_{\mathrm{et}}^{\mathrm{perar}},k_{\mathrm{proet}}^{\mathrm{indrat}}
if
X=\Spec k
with
k
a perfect field of characteristic
p>0
.

Define
\Sh_{\mathrm{fin}}(S_{X})
(resp.
\Sh_{\ell}(S_{X})
for
\ell
a prime) as the full subcategory of
\Sh(S_{X})
of sheaves representable by finite étale (resp. and
\ell
-primary)
X
-groups. Define
D_{\mathrm{fin}}(S_{X})
(resp.
D_{\ell}(S_{X})
) as the full subcategory of
D(S_{X})
of bounded objects with cohomology in
\Sh_{\mathrm{fin}}(S_{X})
(resp. in
\Sh_{\ell}(S_{X})
).

It is clear that
\Sh_{\mathrm{fin}}(S_{X})=\bigoplus_{\ell}\Sh_{\ell}(S_{X})
and
D_{\mathrm{fin}}(S_{X})=\bigoplus_{\ell}D_{\ell}(S_{X})
. The following lemma indicates that these categories contain the same data regardless of the choice of site.

Lemma 56
.

Consider
X
and
S_{X}
as in any case of Definition
55
and
\ell
a prime.

1.

\Sh_{\mathrm{fin}}(S_{X})
(resp.
\Sh_{\ell}(S_{X})
) is an abelian subcategory of
\Sh(S_{X})
closed under extension.
D_{\mathrm{fin}}(S_{X})
(resp.
D_{\ell}(S_{X})
) is the full triangulated subcategory of
D(S_{X})
generated by objects of
\Sh_{\mathrm{fin}}(S_{X})
(resp.
\Sh_{\ell}(S_{X})
) in degree
0
.

2.

Consider
u:S_{X}\to X_{\mathrm{et}}
the morphism of sites defined by identity. Then its pullback
u^{*}
defines exact equivalences of abelian and triangulated categories :

\Sh_{\mathrm{fin}}(X_{\mathrm{et}})\xrightarrow{\sim}\Sh_{\mathrm{fin}}(S_{X}),\quad\Sh_{\ell}(X_{\mathrm{et}})\xrightarrow{\sim}\Sh_{\ell}(S_{X}),\quad D_{\mathrm{fin}}(X_{\mathrm{et}})\xrightarrow{\sim}D_{\mathrm{fin}}(S_{X}),\quad D_{\ell}(X_{\mathrm{et}})\xrightarrow{\sim}D_{\ell}(S_{X})

which commute with the Yoneda functor from the category of finite (
\ell
-primary) étale
X
-groups.

Proof of Lemma
56
.

1.
A finite sheaf
G\in\Sh(S_{X})
is representable by an étale group scheme if and only if it is a locally constant for some
n>0
: for
S_{X}=X_{\mathrm{et}},k_{\mathrm{Et}},k_{\mathrm{RP}},k_{\mathrm{RPS}},k_{\mathrm{et}}^{\mathrm{perar}},k^{\mathrm{indrat}}_{\mathrm{et}}
the proof is identical to
[
34
]
(Lem. 03RV) and for
k^{\mathrm{indrat}}_{\mathrm{proet}}
it follows from an argument identical to
[
34
]
(Lem. 099Y). Clearly finite (resp.
\ell
-primary) locally constant sheaves form an abelian subcategory closed under extensions, and it follows that
D_{\mathrm{fin}}(S_{X})
(resp.
D_{\ell}(S_{X})
) is a triangulated subcategory of
D(S_{X})
, which is clearly the smallest such containing objects of
\Sh_{\mathrm{fin}}(S_{X})
(resp.
\Sh_{\ell}(S_{X})
) in degree
0
.

2.
Note that
u
is indeed a morphism of sites (not just a premorphism) because
X_{\mathrm{et}}
admits all finite products and
u^{-1}
preserves them. By Lemma
11
,
u^{*}:\Sh(X_{\mathrm{et}})\to\Sh(S_{X})
commutes with the Yoneda functors from the category of finite étale
X
-groups (since
u^{-1}:X_{\mathrm{et}}\to S_{X}
is identity). Thus
u^{*}
restricts to
\Sh_{\mathrm{fin}}(X_{\mathrm{et}})\to\Sh_{\mathrm{fin}}(S_{X})
.

The unit map
F\to u_{*}u^{*}(F)
is an isomorphism for all
F\in\Sh(X_{\mathrm{et}})
, because
u
is defined by the inclusion
X_{\mathrm{et}}\to S_{X}
. It follows that
u^{*}:\Sh(X_{\mathrm{et}})\to\Sh(S_{X})
is fully faithful. Its restriction
\Sh_{\mathrm{fin}}(X_{\mathrm{et}})\to\Sh_{\mathrm{fin}}(S_{X})
is essentially surjective by compatibility with the Yoneda functors, thus an equivalence. The pushforward
u_{*}:\Sh(S_{X})\to\Sh(X_{\mathrm{et}})
admits
u^{*}
as left adjoint, thus it preserves injectives, and it is clearly exact. Thus by derivation of the identity
u_{*}u^{*}=\mathrm{id}
and of the composite functor
\Hom_{S_{X}}(u^{*}F,-)=\Hom_{X_{\mathrm{et}}}(F,u_{*}(-))
, we have :

R\Hom_{X_{\mathrm{et}}}(F,G)=R\HOM_{X_{\mathrm{et}}}(F,Ru_{*}u^{*}G)=R\Hom_{S_{X}}(u^{*}F,u^{*}G)

for
F,G\in\Sh(X_{\mathrm{et}})
. Hence
u^{*}:D_{\mathrm{fin}}(X_{\mathrm{et}})\to D(S_{X})
is fully faithful. We conclude that it is an equivalence
D_{\mathrm{fin}}(X_{\mathrm{et}})\cong D_{\mathrm{fin}}(S_{X})
by an argument identical to that in the proof of Proposition
23
.
∎

Recall that for
r\in\mathbb{Z}
and
X
a
\mathbb{Z}[1/m]
-scheme, with
m>0
an integer, we define the Tate twist
\mathbb{Z}/m(r)\in D(S_{X})
as the sheaf
\mu_{m}^{\otimes r}
concentrated in degree
0
; in particular
\mathbb{Z}/m(0)=\mathbb{Z}/m
and
\mathbb{Z}/m(r)
is an invertible
\mathbb{Z}/m
-module with inverse
\mathbb{Z}/m(-r)=\HOM_{S_{X}}(\mathbb{Z}/m,\mathbb{Z}/m(r))
. For
\ell
a prime invertible on
X
, define
\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}/\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(r)=\varinjlim_{n}\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n}(r)
. If
X
is a
\mathbb{Q}
-scheme, define
\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z}(r)=\bigoplus_{\ell}\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}/\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(r)=\varinjlim_{n}\mathbb{Z}/m(r)
.

Proposition 57
.

Consider
X
and
S_{X}
as in any case of Definition
55
. Let
\ell
be a prime invertible on
X
and
d
a fixed integer. Consider the functor
(-)^{\vee}_{S_{X}}=R\HOM_{S_{X}}(-,\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}/\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(d))
on
D(S_{X})
.

1.

For
G\in D_{\ell}(S_{X})
, we have
H^{q}(G^{\vee}_{S_{X}})=\HOM_{S_{X}}(H^{-q}(G),\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}/\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(d))
for
q\in\mathbb{Z}
, and
G^{\vee}_{S_{X}}\in D_{\ell}(S_{X})
.

2.

For any
G\in D_{\ell}(S_{X})
, the canonical map
G\to(G^{\vee}_{S_{X}})^{\vee}_{S_{X}}
is an isomorphism in
D(S_{X})
.

3.

Consider
u:S_{X}\to X_{\mathrm{et}}
the premorphism of sites defined by identity. We have a commutative square :

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D_{\ell}(X_{\mathrm{et}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D_{\ell}(X_{\mathrm{et}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D_{\ell}(S_{X})^{\mathrm{op}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D_{\ell}(S_{X})^{\mathrm{op}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)^{\vee}_{X_{\mathrm{et}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)^{\vee}_{S_{X}}}

4.

If
X
is a
\mathbb{Q}
-scheme, the above holds with
\Sh_{\mathrm{fin}}
,
D_{\mathrm{fin}}
,
\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z}(d)
instead of
\Sh_{\ell}
,
D_{\ell}
,
\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}/\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(d)
.

We say that
G\in\Sh_{\ell}(S_{X})
and
\HOM_{S_{X}}(G,\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}/\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(d))
satisfy a perfect Cartier duality of finite étale groups.

Proof of Proposition
57
.

1.
Because
\ell
is invertible on
X
, the sheaf has injective stalks
\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}/\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}
at geometric points. Thus
\HOM_{S_{X}}(-,\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}/\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(d))
is exact, giving the desired equality. Consider
G\in\Sh_{\ell}(S_{X})
of
\ell^{n}
-torsion, and an étale covering
\{U_{i}\}_{i}
which trivializes
G
and
\mu_{\ell^{n}}
. The sheaf
\HOM_{S_{X}}(G,\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}/\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(d))=\HOM_{S_{X}}(G,\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n}(d))
is trivialized by this étale covering and thus is locally constant. By the same argument as Lemma
56
.1, this sheaf belongs to
\Sh_{\ell}(S_{X})
. Hence
(-)^{\vee}_{S_{X}}
sends objects of
D_{\ell}(S_{X})
to
D_{\ell}(S_{X})
.

2.
For any
n>0
we have :

\HOM_{S_{X}}(\HOM_{S_{X}}(\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n},\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}/\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(d)),\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}/\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(d))=\HOM_{S_{X}}(\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n}(d),\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}/\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}^{n}(d))=\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n}

meaning
G\to(G^{\vee}_{S_{X}})^{\vee}_{S_{X}}
is an isomorphism when
G=\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n}
. If
G\in\Sh_{\ell}(S_{X})
is general, then étale-locally we have
G\cong\bigoplus_{i=1}^{r}\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n_{r}}
for some integers
n_{1},\dots,n_{r}\geq 1
thus
G\to(G^{\vee}_{S_{X}})^{\vee}_{S_{X}}
is again an isomorphism. The same follows for
G\in D_{\ell}(S_{X})
by Lemma
56
.1.

3.
For any
n>0
we have :

u^{*}\HOM_{X_{\mathrm{et}}}(\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n},\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}/\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(d))=u^{*}\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n}(d)=\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n}(d)=(\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n})^{\vee}_{S_{X}}=(u^{*}\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n})^{\vee}_{S_{X}}

by Lemma
56
.2. Hence
u^{*}(G^{\vee}_{X_{\mathrm{et}}})=u^{*}(G)^{\vee}_{S_{X}}
when
G=\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n}
, and as above this generalizes to arbitrary
G\in D_{\mathrm{fin}}(X_{\mathrm{et}})
.

4.
This is clear from
\Sh_{\mathrm{fin}}(S_{X})=\bigoplus_{\ell}\Sh_{\ell}(S_{X})
,
D_{\mathrm{fin}}(S_{X})=\bigoplus_{\ell}D_{\ell}(S_{X})
and
\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z}(d)=\bigoplus_{\ell}\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}/\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(d)
.
∎

The following lemma will not be used in this section, but will appear in the next part.

Lemma 58
.

Let
K
be a field and
S_{K}
be one of the sites of Definition
55
. Let
\ell\neq\characteristic K
be a prime. For any
G\in\Sh_{\ell}(S_{K})
there exists a finite extension
L/K
such that the following holds, where
S_{L}
is the analogous site and
\pi_{L/K}:S_{L}\to S_{K}
the morphism induced by base change.

1.

The degree
[L:K]
is prime to
p
.

2.

There exists a finite separated filtration of
G|_{L}
in
\Sh_{\ell}(S_{L})
with nontrivial quotients
\mathbb{Z}/\ell
.

3.

There unit morphism
G\to\pi_{L/K,*}(G|_{L})
is a split monomorphism
\Sh_{\ell}(S_{L})
.

Proof of Lemma
58
.

We reproduce the argument in the proof of
[
2
]
(Prop. 9.6). By Lemma
56
.2,
G
can be viewed as a Galois module over
K
. Let
L^{\prime}/K
be a finite Galois extension which contains a primitive
\ell
-th root of unity, over which
G
is a trivial Galois module, and
L/K
be a subextension such that
\Gal(L^{\prime}/L)
is a
\ell
-Sylow of
\Gal(L^{\prime}/K)
.

Then
\Gal(L^{\prime}/L)
is a finite
\ell
-group and
G|_{L}
is a finite
\ell
-primary
\Gal(L^{\prime}/L)
-module, so
G|_{L}
either is zero or has a nonzero
\Gal(L^{\prime}/L)
-fixed submodule, in which case it admits a submodule isomorphic to
\mathbb{Z}/\ell
in
\Sh_{\ell}(S_{L})
. Inductively (reasoning the same way on
G|_{L}/(\mathbb{Z}/\ell)
),
G|_{L}
admits a
\Gal(L^{\prime}/L)
-module filtration with graded objects isomorphic to
\mathbb{Z}/\ell
. Because
G|_{L}
is finite, this filtration is bounded, proving
(1.)
.

The composite of the unit and counit maps
G\xrightarrow{\mathrm{res}}\pi_{L/K,*}(G|_{L})\xrightarrow{\mathrm{cores}}G
is multiplication by
[L:K]
. The construction of
L
ensures
[L:K]
is prime to
\ell
, thus
\mathrm{res}
is a split monomorphism with section
[L:K]^{-1}\mathrm{cores}
, proving
(2.)
.
∎

Remark 59
.

The above Lemma
58
appears in the proof of
[
2
]
(Prop. 9.6) for
p
-primary
G
, in the case where
K
is a Henselian discrete valuation field with residue field of charcateristic
p
(there our
L^{\prime}
and
L
are written
L
and
M
). In particular, the residue field extension
l/k
has degree prime to
p
hence is separable. Later in the same proof, they use the Weil restriction
\mathrm{Res}_{l/k}:\Sh(l_{\mathrm{RPS}})\to\Sh(k_{\mathrm{RPS}})
,
i.e.
the pushforward of the premorphism defined by base change, and the fact that it maps
\Sh_{0}(l_{\mathrm{RPS}})
to
\Sh_{0}(k_{\mathrm{RPS}})
. The well-definition of the premorphism
l_{\mathrm{RPS}}\to k_{\mathrm{RPS}}
, and stability of RPAU groups under
\mathrm{Res}_{l/k}
, are easily checked in the case where
l/k
is separable, which covers the use case of
[
2
]
(see
[
19
]
Cor. 1.9,
[
2
]
Prop. 2.5, or Lemma
5
.2). However this remains true if
l/k
is any finite extension  ; this is Lemma
5
.5 and Corollary
29
.

4
Duality for higher local fields

4.1
Positive equal characteristic step

Recall the definition of the Kato lift
h^{K/k}(T)
from Proposition
89
and Definition
91
.

Situation 60
.

Let
K
be a complete discrete valuation field of characteristic
p>0
with residue field
k
. Assume
k
is perfect. Set
S_{K}^{p}=K_{\mathrm{RP}}
and
S_{k}^{p}=k_{\mathrm{et}}^{\mathrm{perar}}
. Define a premorphism of sites
\psi_{K/k}^{p}:S_{K}^{p}\to S_{k}^{p}
by :

(\psi_{K/k}^{p})^{-1}:T/k\mapsto h^{K/k}(T).

We write
\Psi_{K/k}^{p}:\Sh(S_{K}^{p})\to\Sh(S_{k}^{p})
the pushforward of
\psi_{K/k}^{p}
.

The objects
K,k,\psi_{K/k}^{p}
and
\Psi_{K/k}^{p}
correspond to
k,F,\pi_{k,\mathrm{RP}}
and
\pi_{k,\mathrm{RP},*}
in the notations of
[
40
]
§4. The superscript
(-)^{p}
stands for "characteristic
p
", and will be omitted from the notation when no confusion is possible. The following results are from
[
40
]
§4. Recall Serre duality per Theorem
26
and Theorem
43
.

For
F=K,k
and
G\in\Sh(S_{F})
we write
G_{F^{\mathrm{sep}}}=\varinjlim_{E/F}\Gamma(E,G)
the étale stalk at the separable closure
F^{\mathrm{sep}}
(where
E/F
runs over finite separable extensions). We view this stalk as a Galois module over
F
, that is an object of
\Sh(F_{\mathrm{et}})
; this defines an exact functor
(-)_{F^{\mathrm{sep}}}:\Sh(S_{F})\to\Sh(F_{\mathrm{et}})
. For either
F=K,k
which are characteristic
p>0
, we mean
\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}(r)=\nu_{n}(r)[-r]\in D(S_{F})
and
\mathbb{Q}_{p}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}(r)=\nu_{\infty}(r)[-r]
as in section
1.5
.

Theorem 61
.

Consider Situation
60
.

1.

R\Psi_{K/k}
sends
D_{0}(S_{K})
to
D^{b}_{\mathcal{W}_{k}}(S_{k})
. If
G\in\Sh_{0}(S_{K})
then
R^{q}\Psi_{K/k}(G)=0
for
q\neq 0,1
.

2.

There exists a canonical trace map
\mathrm{tr}:R\Psi_{K/k}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}(1))\to\mathbb{Q}_{p}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}[-1]
in
D(S_{k})
.

3.

For any
G\in D_{0}(S_{K})
with Serre dual
G^{\vee}=R\HOM_{S_{K}}(G,\mathbb{Q}_{p}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}(1))
, we have a perfect pairing :

R\Psi_{K/k}(G)\otimes^{L}R\Psi_{K/k}(G^{\vee})\to R\Psi_{K/k}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}(1))\xrightarrow{\mathrm{tr}}\mathbb{Q}_{p}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}[-1]

which gives perfect Serre dualities of connected and finite étale parts for
q\in\mathbb{Z}
:

\displaystyle R^{q}\Psi_{K/k}(G)^{0}

\displaystyle=\EXT^{1}_{S_{k}}(R^{1-q}\Psi_{K/k}(G^{\vee})^{0},\mathbb{Q}_{p}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}),

\displaystyle\pi_{0}(R^{q}\Psi_{K/k}(G))

\displaystyle=\HOM_{S_{k}}(\pi_{0}(R^{-q}\Psi_{K/k}(G^{\vee})),\mathbb{Q}_{p}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}).

4.

For
G\in\Sh(S_{K})
and
q\in\mathbb{Z}
,
R^{q}\Psi_{K/k}(G)
is the sheafification of the presheaf
T\in S_{k}\mapsto H^{q}(h^{K/k}(T),G)
. In particular we have
R\Psi_{K/k}(G)_{k^{\mathrm{sep}}}=R\Gamma(K^{\mathrm{ur}},G_{K^{\mathrm{sep}}})
in
D(k_{\mathrm{et}})
for
G\in D(S_{K})
, where
K^{\mathrm{ur}}=\bigcup_{l/k\text{ finite}}h^{K/k}(l)
is the maximal unramified extension of
K
.

Proof of Theorem
61
.

1.
If
G\in\Sh_{0}(S_{K})
then we have
R^{q}\Psi_{K/k}(G)=0
if
q\neq 0,1
and
R^{q}\Psi_{K/k}(G)\in\mathcal{W}_{k}
by
[
40
]
(Prop. 4.1 for
q=0
, Prop. 4.3 for
q\neq 0,1
, Prop. 4.6 for
q=1
; our
(K,k,\Psi_{K/k})
corresponds precisely to
(k,F,\pi_{k,\mathrm{RP},*})
in that paper  ; note that this reference uses Lemma
37
). For
G\in D_{0}(S_{K})
, the spectral sequence :

E^{ij}_{2}=R^{i}\Psi_{K/k}(H^{j}(G))\Rightarrow R^{i+j}\Psi_{K/k}(G)

gives short exact sequences
0\to R^{1}\Psi_{K/k}(H^{q-1}(G))\to R^{q}\Psi_{K/k}(G)\to R^{0}\Psi_{K/k}(H^{q}(G))\to 0
so by the case
G\in\Sh_{0}(S_{K})
and Lemma
37
,
R^{q}\Psi_{K/k}(G)
belongs to
\mathcal{W}_{k}
and is trivial whenever
H^{q}(G)=H^{q-1}(G)=0
, which is true for all but finitely many
q\in\mathbb{Z}
. Thus
R\Psi_{K/k}(G)\in D^{b}_{\mathcal{W}_{k}}(S_{k})
.

2.
By
[
39
]
(Prop. 6.1.6), we have identifications
R^{q}\Psi_{K/k}(\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}(1))=0
if
q\neq 1
, and
R^{1}\Psi_{K/k}(\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}(1))=R^{0}\Psi_{K/k}(\mathbb{G}_{m,K}^{\mathrm{RP}})/p^{n}
. The latter is the sheafification of the presheaf

F\in k^{\mathrm{perar}}_{\mathrm{et}}\mapsto h^{K/k}(F)^{\times}/p^{n}
. Proposition
95
.2 gives a valuation map
R^{0}\Psi_{K/k}(\mathbb{G}_{m,k}^{\mathrm{RP}})\to\mathbb{Z}
which defines
R^{1}\Psi_{K/k}(\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}(1))\to\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}
for
n\geq 1
.

3.
This is
[
40
]
(Prop. 4.4).

4.
The first statement is by Lemma
53
. The
k^{\mathrm{sep}}
-stalks before and after sheafification coincide so that :

R^{q}\Psi_{K/k}(G)_{k^{\mathrm{sep}}}=\varinjlim_{l/k}H^{q}(h^{K/k}(l)_{\mathrm{et}},G)=H^{q}(\varinjlim_{l/k}h^{K/k}(l)_{\mathrm{et}},G)=H^{q}(K^{\mathrm{ur}}_{\mathrm{et}},G)

for
q\in\mathbb{Z}
and
G\in\Sh(S_{K})
. The same follows for
G\in D(S_{K})
because the functor
(-)_{k^{\mathrm{sep}}}
is exact.
∎

Remark 62
.

By Proposition
41
.3-4 and Proposition
43
.3, the change of site
\alpha_{k}:D(S_{k})\to D(S_{k}^{\prime})
where
S_{k}^{\prime}=k_{\mathrm{proet}}^{\mathrm{indrat}}
, preserves representability by objects of
\mathcal{W}_{k}
, Serre duality and cohomology for such objects. Thus all of Theorem
60
.1-3 remains true if we replace
S_{k}
by
S_{k}^{\prime}
and
R\Psi_{K/k}
by
\alpha_{k}R\Psi_{K/k}
. Theorem
60
.4 remains partially true in that the equality
(\alpha_{k}R\Psi_{K/k}(G))_{k^{\mathrm{sep}}}=R\Gamma(K^{\mathrm{ur}},G_{K^{\mathrm{sep}}})
holds for
G\in D_{0}(S_{K})
.

4.2
Mixed characteristic step

Situation 63
.

Let
K
be a Henselian discrete valuation field with residue field
k
. Assume
K
has mixed characteristic
(0,p)
and
[k:k^{p}]=p^{d}
for some finite
d
. Set
S_{K}^{m}=K_{\mathrm{Et}}
,
S_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}^{m}=\mathcal{O}_{K,\mathrm{RPS}}
and
S_{k}^{m}=k_{\mathrm{RPS}}
. Consider the immersions
j:\Spec K\to j\Spec\mathcal{O}_{K}
and
i:\Spec k\to\Spec\mathcal{O}_{K}
and the premorphisms
j:S_{K}^{m}\to S_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}^{m}
and
i:S_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}^{m}\to S_{k}^{m}
defined by base change. Define
R\Psi_{K/k}^{m}:D(S_{K})\to D(S_{k})
as the composite
R\Psi_{K/k}^{m}=i^{*}Rj_{*}
.

The superscript
(-)^{m}
stands for "mixed characteristic", and as before will often be omitted from notation. This definition of
R\Psi_{K/k}^{m}
connects it to the theory of nearby cycles, whence the notation stems. We abusively retained similar notation in other cases (even ones that have no clear relation to nearby cycles), to highlight the similarities of each setup.

As previously, we write
(-)_{F}:D(S_{F})\to D(F_{\mathrm{et}})
the étale stalk functor for
F=K,k
. Mind that over
S_{K}
where
\characteristic K=0
,
\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}(r)
denotes the sheaf
\mu_{p^{n}}^{\otimes r}
(as in section
3.4
), but over
S_{k}
where
\characteristic k=p
we mean
\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}(r)=\nu_{n}(r)[-r]
(as in section
1.5
), and similarly for
\mathbb{Q}_{p}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}(r)
.

Situation
63
can be unified with Situation
60
as follows, and be given an arithmetic interpretation thus.

Lemma 64
.

Consider Situation
63
. Let
\hat{K}
be the completion of
K
. Define :

—

\tilde{S}_{k}^{m}
the site
k_{\mathrm{RPS},\mathrm{aff}}
of Remark
8
;

—

u_{k}:S_{k}^{m}\to\tilde{S}_{k}^{m}
the premorphism of sites defined by identity on the underlying categories  ;

—

\psi_{K/k}^{m}:S_{K}^{m}\to\tilde{S}_{k}^{m}
the premorphism of sites defined by
(\psi_{K/k}^{m})^{-1}(T)=h^{\hat{K}/k}(T)
for
T\in\tilde{S}_{k}^{m}
;

—

R\tilde{\Psi}_{K/k}^{m}:D(S_{K}^{m})\to D(\tilde{S}_{k}^{m})
the derived pushforward of
\psi_{K/k}^{m}
.

Then we have the following.

1.

For
G\in\Sh(S_{K}^{m})
and
q\in\mathbb{Z}
,
R^{q}\tilde{\Psi}_{K/k}^{m}(G)
is the sheafification of the presheaf
T/k\mapsto H^{q}(h^{\hat{K}/k}(T),G)
.

2.

We have
u_{k,*}R\Psi_{K/k}^{m}=R\tilde{\Psi}_{K/k}^{m}
. In particular for
T\in\tilde{S}_{k}^{m}
,
R\Gamma(T,R\Psi_{K/k}^{m}(-))=R\Gamma(T,R\tilde{\Psi}_{K/k}^{m}(-))
.

3.

We have
R\Psi_{K/k}^{m}(G)_{k^{\mathrm{sep}}}=R\Gamma(K^{\mathrm{ur}},G_{K^{\mathrm{sep}}})
for
G\in D(S_{K}^{m})
where
K^{\mathrm{ur}}=\bigcup_{k^{\mathrm{sep}}/l/k\text{ finite}}h^{K/k}(l)
is the maximal unramified extension of
K
.

The premorphism
\psi_{K/k}^{m}
cannot be defined to target all of
S_{k}^{m}
because the infinite-level Kato lift does not generalize to non-affine schemes (see Remark
92
.2). We could work with
\tilde{S}_{k}^{m}
and
R\tilde{\Psi}_{K/k}^{m}
from the beginning in Situation
63
, and for our purposes no issue would come up (see also Remark
8
).

Proof of Lemma
64
.

Note that
u_{k,*}
is exact because coverings of in
S_{k}^{m}
of objects of
\tilde{S}_{k}^{m}
are refined by coverings in
\tilde{S}_{k}^{m}
. Point
(1.)
is immediate from Lemma
53
. Point
(2.)
follows from the exactness of
u_{k,*}
,
(1.)
and
[
21
]
(Cor. 3.3  ; though stated for sheaves of
\mathbb{Z}/p
-modules, the same proof works for abelian sheaves). Taking the colimit over finite separable extensions
T=\Spec l\to\Spec k
in
(2.)
and applying
(1.)
gives :

R\Psi_{K/k}(G)_{k^{\mathrm{sep}}}=\varinjlim_{l/k}R\Gamma(l,R\tilde{\Psi}_{K/k}(G))=\varinjlim_{l/k}R\Gamma(h^{\hat{K}/k}(l),G)=R\Gamma(K^{\mathrm{ur}},G)

which proves
(3.)
.
∎

In the next lemma and theorem,

Lemma 65
.

Consider
k
a field of characteristic
p\geq 0
,
S_{k}
a site and
d\in\mathbb{Z}
as in one of the following :

—

p=0
,
d\in\mathbb{Z}
is arbitrary and
S_{k}=k_{\mathrm{et}}
;

—

p>0
,
k
is perfect,
d=0
and
S_{k}=k_{\mathrm{et}}^{\mathrm{perar}}
;

—

p>0
,
[k:k^{p}]=p^{d}
and
S_{k}
is either
k_{\mathrm{RP}}
or
k_{\mathrm{RPS}}
.

Let
\pi:\Spec l\to\Spec k
be a finite field extension and
S_{l}
the analogous site.

1.

Base change by
\pi
defines a morphism of sites
S_{l}\to S_{k}
, also written
\pi
, where
\pi_{*}:\Sh(S_{l})\to\Sh(S_{k})
and
\pi^{*}\Sh(S_{k})\to\Sh(S_{l})
are both left and right adjoint to each other.

2.

There is an isomorphism
\eta:\pi^{*}\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z}(d)_{k}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z}(d)_{l}
in
D(S_{l})
and a map
\mathrm{Tr}:\pi_{*}\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z}(d)_{l}\to\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z}(d)_{k}
in
D(S_{k})
such that the composite
\mathrm{Tr}\circ\pi_{*}\eta
is the counit
\pi_{*}\pi^{*}\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z}(d)_{k}\to\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z}(d)_{k}
.

3.

If
G\otimes^{L}H\to\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z}(d)_{l}
is a perfect pairing in
D(S_{l})
, then we have a perfect pairing in
D(S_{k})
:

\pi_{*}G\otimes^{L}\pi_{*}H\to\pi_{*}\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z}(d)_{l}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{Tr}}\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z}(d)_{k}.

4.

If
G\otimes^{L}H\to\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z}(d)_{k}
is a pairing in
D(S_{k})
then we have a commutative diagram :

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\otimes^{L}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z}(d)_{k}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{*}\pi^{*}G}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\otimes^{L}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{*}\pi^{*}H}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{*}\pi^{*}\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z}(d)_{k}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{*}\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z}(d)_{l}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z}(d)_{k}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{*}\eta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Tr}}

where the vertical maps
\pi_{*}\pi^{*}\to\mathrm{id}
and
\mathrm{id}\to\pi_{*}\pi^{*}
are the counit and unit.

Proof of Lemma
65
.

1.
If
S_{k}=k_{\mathrm{RP}},k_{\mathrm{RPS}}
, this is Proposition
28
.

If
S_{k}=k_{\mathrm{et}}
then
\pi:S_{l}\to S_{k}
is a well-defined morphism of sites because
S_{k}
has finite fiber products which are preserved by
\pi^{-1}
.

If
S_{k}=k_{\mathrm{et}}^{\mathrm{perar}}
with
k
perfect, then for
R\in k_{\mathrm{et}}^{\mathrm{perar}}
the algebra
R\otimes_{k}l
belongs to
l_{\mathrm{et}}^{\mathrm{perar}}
by
[
37
]
(Prop. 3.3.(b)) so
\pi:l_{\mathrm{et}}^{\mathrm{perar}}\to k_{\mathrm{et}}^{\mathrm{perar}}
is well-defined. As
\pi:\Spec l\to\Spec k
is a covering in
k_{\mathrm{et}}^{\mathrm{perar}}
,
\pi^{\mathrm{set}}
is simply given by restricting a sheaf of sets to the subcategory
S_{l}\subseteq S_{k}
. In particular
\pi^{\mathrm{set}}
is exact.

In either case
S_{k}=k_{\mathrm{et}},k_{\mathrm{et}}^{\mathrm{perar}}
because
k
is perfect, the functor
\pi_{*}
is left adjoint to
\pi^{*}
because
\pi
is a finite étale morphism, by the same proof as
[
25
]
(Lem. V.1.12).

2.
If
\ell\neq p
is a prime, then
\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n}(d)_{k}
is representable by the finite étale
k
-group
\mu_{n}^{\otimes d}
concentrated in degree
0
. By Lemma
11
,
\pi^{*}\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n}(d)_{k}
is representable by the base change of
\mu_{n}^{\otimes d}
to
l
concentrated in degree
0
, which is again representable by
\mu_{n}^{\otimes d}
. Hence we have an isomorphism
\eta_{\ell}:\pi^{*}\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}/\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(d)\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}/\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(d)_{k}
and we can arbitrarily define
\mathrm{Tr}_{\ell}
as the composite
\pi_{*}\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}/\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(d)_{l}\xrightarrow{\sim}\pi_{*}\pi^{*}\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}/\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(d)_{k}\to\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}/\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(d)_{k}
.

In case
S_{k}=k_{\mathrm{et}}
where we assume
p=0
, this is enough to define
\eta=\bigoplus_{\ell}\eta_{\ell}
and
\mathrm{Tr}=\bigoplus_{\ell}\mathrm{Tr}_{\ell}
and conclude. In case
S_{k}=k_{\mathrm{et}}^{\mathrm{perar}}
where
k
is perfect and
d=0
, the group
\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}(d)=\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}
remains finite étale and the same argument applies. In cases
S_{k}=k_{\mathrm{RP}},k_{\mathrm{RPS}}
, the
p
-primary parts
\eta_{p}:\pi^{*}\mathbb{Q}_{p}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}(d)_{k}\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbb{Q}_{p}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}(d)_{l}
and
\mathrm{Tr}_{p}:\pi_{*}\mathbb{Q}_{p}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}(d)_{l}\to\mathbb{Q}_{p}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}(d)_{k}
can be taken as in Theorem
34
.

3.-4.
By
[
34
]
(Lem. 030K) and by construction of
\eta
and
\mathrm{Tr}
, we can assume either
l/k
is separable, or
S_{k}=k_{\mathrm{RP}},k_{\mathrm{RPS}}
and
l/k
is purely inseparable which only occurs in cases
S_{k}=k_{\mathrm{RP}},k_{\mathrm{RPS}}
. These can be treated by the same proofs as Theorem
34
, using
(1.-2.)
.
∎

Remark 66
.

We do not use the full generality of Lemma
65
. In Theorem
68
, the extension
l/k
is prime-to-
p
hence separable, which was easy. In Theorem
76
, we only need consider
\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}/\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(d)
and coefficients
G\in D_{\ell}(S_{l})
,
G,H\in D(S_{k})
;
(2.)
for the prime-to-
p
part was easy and by Lemma
56
, points
(3.-4.)
reduce to claims over small étale sites, which follow from duality for finite étale maps (when
l/k
is separable) and topological invariance (when
l/k
is purely inseparable). The hard case of Lemma
65
,
i.e.
Theorem
34
for
l/k
purely inseparable goes unused, as well as Proposition
31
, Lemma
33
, and the equivalences
l_{\mathrm{RP}}\cong k_{\mathrm{RP}}
and
l_{\mathrm{RPS}}\cong k_{\mathrm{RPS}}
of Lemma
5
.3-4 (though we use Lemma
5
.3-4 to define
\pi:l_{\mathrm{RP}}\to k_{\mathrm{RP}}
and
\pi:l_{\mathrm{RPS}}\to k_{\mathrm{RPS}}
).

Lemma 67
.

Consider
K,k,S_{K},S_{k},R\Psi_{K/k}
as in Situation
63
. Consider
L/K
a finite extension with residue field
l
(which is a finite, possibly not separable extension of
k
). Then one can analogously consider
S_{L},S_{l},R\Psi_{L/l}
as in Situation
63
. There is a canonical isomorphism in
D(S_{k})
:

R\Psi_{K/k}(\pi_{L/K,*}G)=\pi_{l/k,*}(R\Psi_{L/l}G)

natural in
G\in D(S_{L})
, where
\pi_{L/K}:S_{L}\to S_{K}
and
\pi_{l/k}:S_{l}\to S_{k}
are induced by base change.

For this proof we reduce to showing the equality on affine objects of
S_{k}
, which we do using Lemma
64
. We will use this strategy again several times, such as in Lemma
75
and Lemma
83
. We write it out properly here, but in subsequent proofs we will not as rigorously differentiate between
R\Psi_{K/k}^{m}
and
R\tilde{\Psi}_{K/k}^{m}
.

Proof of Lemma
67
.

The functor
\pi_{L/K,*}:\Sh(S_{L})\to\Sh(S_{K})
is exact because
L/K
is finite étale, and the functor
\pi_{l/k,*}:\Sh(S_{l})\to\Sh(S_{k})
is exact by Proposition
28
.2. The commutative diagram :

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Spec L}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Spec\mathcal{O}_{L}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Spec l}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Spec K}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Spec\mathcal{O}_{K}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Spec k}

induces a map
\pi_{l/k,*}R\Psi_{L/l}\to R\Psi_{K/k}\pi_{L/K,*}
. We need to show
R^{q}\Psi_{K/k}(\pi_{L/K,*}G)\to\pi_{l/k,*}(R^{q}\Psi_{L/l}G)
is an isomorphism for
q\in\mathbb{Z}
and
G\in\Sh(S_{L})
. Both sides are Zariski sheaves, so it suffices to show they coincide on affine objects of
S_{k}
. By Lemma
64
.2, it suffices to show
R^{q}\tilde{\Psi}_{K/k}(\pi_{L/K,*}G)=\pi_{l/k,*}(R^{q}\tilde{\Psi}_{L/l}G)

By Lemma
64
.1, for
(E,F)=(K,k),(L,l)
we have
R^{q}\tilde{\Psi}_{E/F}=a_{F}\circ P^{q}_{E/F}
, where
a_{F}
is sheafification over
\tilde{S}_{F}
and
P^{q}_{E/F}(H)\in\mathrm{PSh}(\tilde{S}_{F})
is the presheaf
T\mapsto H^{q}(h^{E/F}(T),H)
, for
H\in\Sh(S_{F})
. By
[
2
]
(Lem. 9.7.2)
\pi_{E/F,*}\circ a_{E}=a_{F}\circ\pi_{E/F,p}
where
\pi_{E/F,p}
is the pushforward of presheaves. For
T=\Spec R\in\tilde{S}_{k}
:

\pi_{l/k,p}P^{q}_{L/l}(G)(T)=H^{q}(h^{L/l}(R\otimes_{k}l),G)=H^{q}(h^{K/k}(R)\otimes_{K}L,G)=P^{q}_{K/k}(\pi_{L/K,*}G)(T)

by Lemma
97
. By applying
a_{k}
we get :
\pi_{l/k,*}R^{q}\tilde{\Psi}_{L/l}(G)=R^{q}\tilde{\Psi}_{K/k}(\pi_{L/K,*}G)

which concludes.
∎

The following theorem is by
[
21
]
for coefficients
\mathbb{Z}/p^{n}(r)
; we generalize to finite coefficients. Recall Cartier and Serre duality from Proposition
57
and Theorem
26
.

Theorem 68
.

Consider Situation
63
. Recall that here,
d=\log_{p}[k:k^{p}]
is finite.

1.

R\Psi_{K/k}
sends
D_{p}(S_{K})
to
D_{0}(S_{k})
. For
G\in\Sh_{p}(S_{K})
, we have
R^{q}\Psi_{K/k}(G)=0
if
q\neq 0,\dots,d+1
.

2.

There is a canonical trace map
R\Psi_{K/k}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}(d+1))\to\mathbb{Q}_{p}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}(d)[-1]
in
D(S_{k})
.

3.

For
G\in D_{p}(S_{K})
and
G^{\vee}=R\HOM_{S_{K}}(G,\mathbb{Q}_{p}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}(d+1))
, there is a perfect pairing in
D(S_{k})
:

R\Psi_{K/k}(G)\otimes^{L}R\Psi_{K/k}(G^{\vee})\to R\Psi_{K/k}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}(d+1))\xrightarrow{\mathrm{tr}}\mathbb{Q}_{p}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}(d)[-1]

which gives perfect Serre dualities of split and wound parts for
q\in\mathbb{Z}
:

\displaystyle R^{q}\Psi_{K/k}(G)_{s}

\displaystyle=\EXT_{S_{k}}^{1}(R^{d+1-q}\Psi_{K/k}(G^{\vee}_{K})_{s},\nu_{\infty}(d)),

\displaystyle R^{q}\Psi_{K/k}(G)_{w}

\displaystyle=\HOM_{S_{k}}(R^{d-q}\Psi_{K/k}(G^{\vee}_{K})_{w},\nu_{\infty}(d)).

Proof of Theorem
68
.

1.
Consider
G\in\Sh_{p}(S_{K})
, and let
L/K
be as in Lemma
58
, for
G
and the prime
p\neq\characteristic K
. Let
l/k
be the residue extension. Consider
S_{L},S_{l},R\Psi_{L/l}
as in Situation
63
analogously.

The subcategory of objects
H\in\Sh_{p}(S_{L})
such that
R^{q}\Psi_{L/l}(H)
belongs to
\Sh_{0}(S_{l})
for all
q\in\mathbb{Z}
, and is trivial for
q\neq 0,\dots,d+1
, is closed under extensions by Proposition
9
and exactness of
R\Psi_{L/l}
. The constant sheaf
\mathbb{Z}/p
belongs to this subcategory by
[
21
]
(Th. 3.4.2). Thus
G|_{L}
, which admits a finite separated filtration with quotients
0
or
\mathbb{Z}/p
, also belongs to that category.

Write
P=\pi_{L/K,*}(G|_{L})
. By Lemma
58
.3 there is a decomposition
P=G\oplus H
for some
H\in\Sh_{p}(S_{K})
, so we have
R^{q}\Psi_{K/k}(P)=R^{q}\Psi_{K/k}(G)\oplus R^{q}\Psi_{K/k}(H)
. By Lemma
67
,
R^{q}\Psi_{K/k}(P)=\pi_{l/k,*}R^{q}\Psi_{L/l}(G|_{L})
, so
R^{q}\Psi_{K/k}(P)
is trivial for
q\neq 0,\dots,d+1
, and belongs to
\Sh_{0}(S_{k})
by the above and Corollary
29
, for all
q\in\mathbb{Z}
. As a direct factor of
R^{q}\Psi_{K/k}(P)
,
R^{q}\Psi_{K/k}(G)=0
if
q\neq 0,\dots,d+1
and for any
q\in\mathbb{Z}
is the kernel of some endomorphism of
R^{q}\Psi_{K/k}(P)
, thus belongs to
\Sh_{0}(S_{k})
because
\Sh_{0}(S_{k})
is abelian.

2.
This is
[
21
]
(Th. 3.4.1), using the convention
\mathbb{Q}_{p}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}(d)=\nu_{\infty}(d)[-d]
over
k
.

3.
We can take
G\in\Sh_{p}(S_{K})
by exactness of
R\Psi_{K/k}
and
(-)^{\vee}
. Let
L/K
and
l/k
be as in
(1.)
, then :

R\Psi_{L/l}(H)\otimes^{L}R\Psi_{L/l}(H^{\vee}_{L})\to R\Psi_{L/l}(\mathbb{Q}_{p}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}(d+1))\xrightarrow{\mathrm{tr}}\mathbb{Q}_{p}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}(d)[-1]

is a perfect pairing for
H=\mathbb{Z}/p
, by
[
21
]
(Th. 3.4.3). By exactness of
(-)^{\vee}_{L}=R\HOM_{S_{L}}(-,\mathbb{Q}_{p}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}(d+1))
and
R\Psi_{L/l}
, it is also perfect for
H=G|_{L}
which has a finite separated filtration with quotients
0
or
\mathbb{Z}/p
.

Write
P=\pi_{L/K,*}(G|_{L})
. The pairing :

R\Psi_{K/k}(P)\otimes^{L}R\Psi_{K/k}(P^{\vee}_{K})\to\mathbb{Q}_{p}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}(d)[-1]

is perfect in
D(S_{l})
by Lemma
67
and Lemma
65
.3 for the extension
l/k
. By Lemma
58
.3, there is a decomposition
P=G\oplus H
for some
H\in\Sh_{p}(S_{K})
, so
R\Psi_{K/k}(P)=R\Psi_{K/k}(G)\oplus R\Psi_{K/k}(H)
in
D(S_{k})
. Similarly
P^{\vee}_{K}=G^{\vee}_{K}\oplus H^{\vee}_{K}
and
R\Psi_{K/k}(P^{\vee}_{K})=R\Psi_{K/k}(G^{\vee}_{K})\oplus R\Psi_{K/k}(H^{\vee}_{K})
, and the previous pairing decomposes as the orthogonal direct sum of the corresponding pairings :

R\Psi_{K/k}(G)\otimes^{L}R\Psi_{K/k}(G^{\vee}_{K})\to\mathbb{Q}_{p}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}(d)[-1],\qquad R\Psi_{K/k}(H)\otimes^{L}R\Psi_{K/k}(H^{\vee}_{K})\to\mathbb{Q}_{p}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}(d)[-1]

by Lemma
65
.4. Hence both are also perfect in
D(S_{k})
. The rest follows from Theorem
26
.5.
∎

Remark 69
.

By Lemma
56
.2 and Proposition
57
.4, the morphism of sites
u:S_{K}\to S_{K}^{\prime}
where
S_{K}^{\prime}=K_{\mathrm{et}}
, induces an exact equivalence
u^{*}:D_{p}(S_{K}^{\prime})\xrightarrow{\sim}D_{p}(S_{K})
which commutes with the Cartier duality and Yoneda functors from the category of
p
-primary finite étale
K
-groups. Similarly, by Proposition
12
and Proposition
23
, the obvious premorphism of sites
v:S_{k}^{\prime}\to S_{k}
where
S_{k}^{\prime}=k_{\mathrm{RP}}
induces an exact equivalence
v^{*}:D_{0}(S_{k})\xrightarrow{\sim}D_{0}(S_{k}^{\prime})
which commutes with the Serre duality and Yoneda functors from
D^{b}(\Alg_{u}^{\mathrm{RP}}(k))
. Thus all of Theorem
68
, as well as Lemma
64
.3 remain true if we replace
S_{K}
by
S_{K}^{\prime}
and/or
S_{k}
by
S_{k}^{\prime}
, and accordingly replace
R\Psi_{K/k}
by its composite on the left with
Lv^{*}
and/or on the right with
u^{*}
.

4.3
Prime-to-characteristic steps

Situation 70
.

Let
k
be a field and
d
any integer. Let
X/k
be a proper, smooth, geometrically integral
k
-scheme of dimension
e\geq 0
. Let
U\subseteq X
be any nonempty open. Set
S_{U}^{v}=U_{\mathrm{et}}
and
S_{k}^{v}=k_{\mathrm{et}}
and define a morphism of sites
\psi_{U/k}^{v}:S_{U}^{v}\to S_{k}^{v}
by :

(\psi_{U/k}^{v})^{-1}:T/k\mapsto U_{T}=U\times_{k}T.

We write
\Psi_{U/k}^{v}:\Sh(S_{U}^{v})\to\Sh(S_{k}^{v})
the pushforward of
\psi_{U/k}^{v}
and
R\Psi_{U/k}^{v}:D(S_{U}^{v})\to D(S_{k}^{v})
its derived functor. We also define
R\Psi_{U/k,c}^{v}=R\Psi_{X/k}^{v}\circ j_{!}
where
j_{!}:D(S_{U}^{v})\to D(S_{X}^{v})
is the exceptional pushforward induced by the open immersion
j:U\to X
.

We will as before often omit the superscript
(-)^{v}
(which stands for "variety"). Note that
\psi_{U/k}^{v}
is a morphism of sites is because
k_{\mathrm{et}}
admits fiber products which are preserved by
(\psi_{U/k}^{v})^{-1}
. As in section
3.4
, over
S_{U}
or
S_{k}
, for
\ell\neq\characteristic k
a prime, by
\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n}(r)
and
\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}/\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(r)
we mean the sheaves
\mu_{\ell^{n}}^{\otimes r}
and
\varinjlim_{n}\mu_{\ell^{n}}^{\otimes r}
.

The following is a reformulation of Poincaré duality with the above notations.

Theorem 71
.

Consider Situation
70
. Let
\ell\neq\characteristic k
be a fixed prime and
d
a fixed integer.

1.

The functors
R\Psi_{U/k}
and
R\Psi_{U/k,c}
send objects of
D_{\ell}(S_{U})
to objects of
D_{\ell}(S_{k})
. If
G\in\Sh_{\ell}(S_{U})
then
R^{q}\Psi_{U/k}(G)=R^{q}\Psi_{U/k,c}(G)=0
for
q\neq 0,\dots,2e
.

2.

There exists a canonical trace map
\mathrm{tr}:R\Psi_{U/k,c}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}/\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(d+e))\to\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}/\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(d)[-2e]
in
D(S_{k})
.

3.

For any
G\in D_{\ell}(S_{U})
with Cartier dual
G^{\vee}=R\HOM_{S_{U}}(G,\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}/\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(d+e))
, we have a perfect pairing :

R\Psi_{U/k}(G)\otimes^{L}R\Psi_{U/k,c}(G^{\vee})\to R\Psi_{U/k,c}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}/\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(d+e))\xrightarrow{\mathrm{tr}}\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}/\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(d)[-2e]

which gives perfect Cartier dualities of finite étale
k
-groups for
q\in\mathbb{Z}
:

R^{q}\Psi_{U/k}(G)\otimes R^{2e-q}\Psi_{U/k,c}(G^{\vee})\to\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}/\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(d).

4.

We have identities
R\Psi_{U/k}(G)=R\Gamma(U_{k^{\mathrm{sep}}},G)
and
R\Psi_{U/k,c}(G)=R\Gamma_{c}(U_{k^{\mathrm{sep}}},G)=R\Gamma(X_{k^{\mathrm{sep}}},j_{k^{\mathrm{sep}},!}G)
for
G\in D(S_{U})
, where we again write
G
for its pullback to other étale sites and the morphism
j_{k^{\mathrm{sep}}}:U_{k^{\mathrm{sep}}}\to X_{k^{\mathrm{sep}}}
is the base change of
j:U\to X
by
\Spec k^{\mathrm{sep}}\to\Spec k
.

Proof of Proposition
71
.

4.
As a complex of sheaves on the étale site of a field,
R\Psi_{U/k}(G)
can be identified with the complex of Galois modules of stalks
R\Psi_{U/k}(G)_{k^{\mathrm{sep}}}
. By Lemma
53
,
R^{q}\Psi_{U/k}(G)
is the sheafification of the presheaf
T/k\mapsto H^{q}(U_{T},G)
for
q\in\mathbb{Z}
, so that :

R^{q}\Psi_{U/k}(G)_{k^{\mathrm{sep}}}=\varinjlim_{l/k}H^{q}(U_{l},G)=H^{q}(\varprojlim_{l/k}U_{l},G)=H^{q}(U_{k^{\mathrm{sep}}},G)

where
l
ranges over finite separable extensions of
k
, by
[
25
]
(Lem. III.1.16). Similarly
R^{q}\Psi_{U/k,c}(G)
is the sheafification of the presheaf
T/k\mapsto H^{q}(X_{T},(j_{!}G)|_{X_{T}})
, so
R^{q}\Psi_{U/k,c}(G)=H^{q}(X_{k^{\mathrm{sep}}},(j_{!}G)|_{X_{k^{\mathrm{sep}}}})
and by smooth base change (
[
34
]
Th. 0EYU),
R^{q}\Psi_{U/k,c}(G)=H^{q}(X_{k^{\mathrm{sep}}},j_{k^{\mathrm{sep}},!}(G|_{X_{k^{\mathrm{sep}}}}))
as desired.

1-3.
By
(4.)
these are reformulations of
[
1
]
(Exposé XVIII, 3.2.6.1) in the case
d=0
, since
U_{k^{\mathrm{sep}}}
is a smooth
k^{\mathrm{sep}}
-variety of dimension
e
by the geometrically integral assumption. Case
d\in\mathbb{Z}
follows from :

\displaystyle\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}/\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(d+e)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}/\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(e)\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}/\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(d)

\displaystyle H^{q}_{c}(U_{k^{\mathrm{sep}}},G\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}/\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(d))

\displaystyle=H^{q}_{c}(U_{k^{\mathrm{sep}}},G)\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}/\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(d)

\displaystyle\HOM_{S_{k}}(G,\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}/\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(d+e))

\displaystyle=\HOM_{S_{k}}(G,\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}/\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(e))\otimes\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}/\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(d)

for arbitrary
G\in\Sh(S_{k})
.
∎

Recall the definition of the étale lift
h^{K/k}(T)
, for
T/k
étale, as a special case of Definition
91
.

Situation 72
.

Let
K
be a complete discrete valuation field with residue field
k
. Assume
K
has equal characteristic
0
. Set
S_{K}^{0}=K_{\mathrm{et}}
and
S_{k}^{0}=k_{\mathrm{et}}
and define a premorphism of sites
\psi_{K/k}^{0}:S_{K}^{0}\to S_{k}^{0}
by :

(\psi_{K/k}^{0})^{-1}:T/k\mapsto h^{K/k}(T).

We write
\Psi_{K/k}^{0}:\Sh(S_{K}^{0})\to\Sh(S_{k}^{0})
the pushforward of
\psi_{K/k}^{0}
.

As before we will usually omit the superscript
(-)^{0}
(which stands for "characteristic
0
").

Duality in Situation
72
is classical. For the purpose of duality for higher local fields, one could use the formalisms of Situation
63
and Situation
60
exclusively for
p
-torsion coefficients and treat
\ell
-torsion coefficients more straightforwardly with just Galois cohomology and small étale sites, which can be treated identically to Situation
72
. In this section we will also show that
R\Psi_{K/k}
satisfies prime-to-
p
duality in Situation
63
and Situation
60
. This is harder than when working directly with Galois cohomology, as can be seen in the proofs of Lemma
75
and Theorem
76
. As noted above this is not strictly needed to obtain Theorem
A
and Theorem
B
, but illustrates the kind of difficulties that appear in these formalisms, and shows that they work uniformly independant of the characteristic. We need preliminary computations. The main tools are Kato and Suzuki’s ind-smooth approximation of the Kato lift (
[
21
]
Prop. 4.1) and Fujiwara-Gabber’s formal base change (
[
3
]
Cor. 1.13.2, here in the form of
[
21
]
4.2).

Lemma 73
.

Let
K,k,S_{K},S_{k},R\Psi_{K/k}
be as in Situation
60
, Situation
63
or Situation
72
. Let
L/K
be a finite separable extension with residue field
l
finite (but not necessarily separable) over
k
, and
S_{L},S_{l},R\Psi_{L/l}
the analogous objects. Then there are canonical isomorphisms for all
q\in\mathbb{Z}
:

R\Psi_{K/k}(\pi_{L/K,*}G)=\pi_{l/k,*}(R\Psi_{L/l}G),\qquad R^{q}\Psi_{K/k}(\pi_{L/K,*}G)=\pi_{l/k,*}(R^{q}\Psi_{L/l}G)

natural in
G\in D(S_{L})
, where
\pi_{L/K}:S_{L}\to S_{K}
and
\pi_{l/k}:S_{l}\to S_{k}
are induced by base change.

Proof of Lemma
73
.

For the mixed characteristic case Situation
63
, see Lemma
67
. The well-definedness of
\pi_{L/K}
and
\pi_{l/k}
and exactness of the pushforward is either by Lemma
28
or properties of finite étale maps. For the other cases, the proof is identical except we do not need Lemma
64
.1 : we directly use Lemma
53
to show
R^{q}\Psi_{E/F}(G)
is the sheafification of
T\mapsto H^{q}(h^{E/F}(T),G)
, for
(E,F)=(K,k),(L,l)
.
∎

Lemma 74
.

Let
n\geq 1
be an integer
i_{0}:Z_{0}\to X_{0}
a closed immersion of regular
\mathbb{Z}[1/n]
-schemes, of codimension
c\geq 0
. Let
\{X_{\alpha}\}_{\alpha\in A}
be a filtered diagram of smooth
X_{0}
-schemes, with affine transition maps, and set
Z_{\alpha}=X_{\alpha}\times_{X_{0}}Z_{0}
,
X=\varprojlim_{\alpha}X_{\alpha}
,
Z=\varprojlim_{\alpha}Z_{\alpha}
. Label the maps as follows :

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z_{\alpha}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{\alpha}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{Z,0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{Z,\alpha}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{Z,\alpha,0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{\alpha}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{X,0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{X,\alpha}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{X,\alpha,0}}

1.

For
F
a sheaf of
\mathbb{Z}/n
-modules on
X_{0,\mathrm{et}}
and
q\in\mathbb{Z}
, we have :

R^{q}i_{\alpha}^{!}f_{X,\alpha,0}^{*}F=f_{Z,\alpha,0}^{*}R^{q}i_{0}^{!}F,\qquad R^{q}i^{!}f_{X,0}^{*}F=f_{Z,0}^{*}R^{q}i_{0}^{!}F=\varinjlim_{\alpha}f_{Z,\alpha}^{*}R^{q}i_{\alpha}^{!}f_{X,\alpha,0}^{*}F.

2.

For all
r\in\mathbb{Z}
we have
R^{q}i^{!}(\mathbb{Z}/n(r))=\mathbb{Z}/n(r-c)
if
q=2c
and
R^{q}i^{!}(\mathbb{Z}/n(r))=0
if
q\neq 2c
.

Proof of Lemma
74
.

1.
We start with
q=0
. Write
j:U\to X
,
j_{\alpha}:U_{\alpha}\to X_{\alpha}
,
j_{0}:U_{0}\to X_{0}
the open complements of
Z
,
Z_{\alpha}
and
Z_{0}
, and
f_{U,\alpha,0}:U_{\alpha}\to U_{0}
,
f_{U,0}:U\to U_{0}
,
f_{U,\alpha}:U\to U_{\alpha}
the obvious maps, so that
U_{\alpha}
and
U
are base changes of
U_{0}
. We have :

\displaystyle i_{\alpha}^{!}f_{X,\alpha,0}^{*}F

\displaystyle=i_{\alpha}^{*}\ker(f_{X,\alpha,0}^{*}F\to j_{\alpha,*}j_{\alpha}^{*}f_{X,\alpha,0}^{*}F)

\displaystyle=\ker(i_{\alpha}^{*}f_{X,\alpha,0}^{*}F\to i_{\alpha}^{*}j_{\alpha,*}j_{\alpha}^{*}f_{X,\alpha,0}^{*}F)

\displaystyle=\ker(f_{Z,\alpha,0}^{*}i_{0}^{*}F\to i_{\alpha}^{*}j_{\alpha,*}f_{U,\alpha,0}^{*}j_{0}^{*}F)

\displaystyle=\ker(f_{Z,\alpha,0}^{*}i_{0}^{*}F\to i_{\alpha}^{*}f_{X,\alpha,0}^{*}j_{0,*}j_{0}^{*}F).

The first equality is by
[
25
]
(Rem. 3.13). The second is by exactness of pullbacks. The third is by functoriality of pullbacks. For the fourth, by smoothness of
X_{\alpha}
we have
f_{X,\alpha,0}^{*}=f_{X,\alpha,0}^{!}(-d)[-2d]
on
\Sh(X_{0,\mathrm{et}},\mathbb{Z}/n)
, where
d
is the (locally constant) dimension of
X_{\alpha}
over
X_{0}
, and because
Rj_{\alpha,*}
and
Rj_{0,*}
commute with the Tate twist of
n
-torsion sheaves, when restricted to
\Sh(X_{0,\mathrm{et}},\mathbb{Z}/n)
we have :

Rj_{\alpha,*}f_{U,\alpha,0}^{*}=(Rj_{\alpha,*}f_{U,\alpha,0}^{!})(-d)[-2d]=(f_{X,\alpha,0}^{!}Rj_{0,*})(-d)[-2d]=f_{X,\alpha,0}^{*}Rj_{0,*}

and
j_{\alpha,*}f_{U,\alpha,0}^{*}=f_{X,\alpha,0}^{*}j_{0,*}
as required. Then by functoriality of pullbacks and exactness of
f_{Z,\alpha,0}^{*}
:

i_{\alpha}^{!}f_{X,\alpha,0}^{*}F=\ker(f_{Z,\alpha,0}^{*}i_{0}^{*}F\to f_{Z,\alpha,0}^{*}i_{0}^{*}j_{0,*}j_{0}^{*}F)=f_{Z,\alpha,0}i_{0}^{!}F

as desired for
q=0
. The claim for
q\geq 1
follows because pullbacks are exact. For
i
, the proof is identical except in the fourth equality we use
[
25
]
(Lem. III.1.16) to have :

Rj_{*}f_{U,0}^{*}=\varinjlim_{\alpha}f_{X,\alpha}^{*}Rj_{\alpha,*}f_{U,\alpha,0}^{*}=\varinjlim_{\alpha}f_{X,\alpha}^{*}f_{X,\alpha,0}^{*}Rj_{0,*}=f_{X,0}^{*}Rj_{0,*}.

2.
This follows from
(1.)
and the absolute purity theorem for the
i_{\alpha}
’s (
[
29
]
(Th. 3.1.1)).
∎

Lemma 75
.

Consider
K,k,S_{K},S_{k},R\Psi_{K/k}
as in any of Situation
60
, Situation
63
or Situation
72
. Let
n\geq 1
be invertible in
k
and
d\in\mathbb{Z}
arbitrary. Then for
r,q\in\mathbb{Z}
we have canonical isomorphisms :

\displaystyle R^{q}\Psi_{K/k}(\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n}(r))

\displaystyle=\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n}(r)\text{ if q=0,}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{Z}/\ell^{n}(r-1)\text{ if q=1,}

\displaystyle=0\text{ otherwise.}

Proof of Lemma
75
.

The (restriction to affines of the) sheaf
R^{q}\Psi_{K/k}(\mathbb{Z}/n(r))\in\Sh(S_{k})
is the sheafification of the presheaf
T\in S_{k}\mapsto H^{q}(h^{K/k}(T),\mathbb{Z}/n(r))
by Lemma
53
in Situation
60
and Situation
72
, and by Lemma
64
in Situation
63
. We have, for
r,s\in\mathbb{Z}
:

H^{q}(h^{K/k}(R),\mathbb{Z}/n(r+s))=H^{q}(h^{K/k}(R),\mathbb{Z}/n(r))\otimes\mathbb{Z}/n(s)

so it suffices to prove the claim for one
r\in\mathbb{Z}
, for each
q\in\mathbb{Z}
. Here is a summary of the following proof.

for
q=0
, we always easily have
\Psi_{K/k}(\mu_{n})=\mu_{n}
for
R\in S_{k}
, essentially by Proposition
95
.2.

For
q=1
, the valuation map gives an injection
\delta:\Psi_{K/k}(\mathbb{G}_{m})/n\to\mathbb{Z}/n
. In Situation
72
and Situation
60
we get surjectivity of
\delta
by Hilbert’s theorem 90 for fields. In Situation
63
it is not clear that
R^{1}\Psi_{K/k}(\mathbb{G}_{m})
is trivial so the same argument doesn’t adapt. Instead we use a localization sequence and compute
H^{q}(h^{\mathcal{O}_{K}/k}(R),\mathbb{Z}/n(r))
and
H^{q}_{\Spec R}(h^{\mathcal{O}_{K}/k}(R),\mathbb{Z}/n(r))
. The first group is computed using the fact that
(h^{\mathcal{O}_{K}/k}(R),\mathfrak{m}_{K}h^{\mathcal{O}_{K}/k}(R))
is Henselian, and the second by locally approximating
h^{\mathcal{O}_{K}/k}(R)
with an ind-smooth
\mathcal{O}_{K}
-algebra and using absolute purity per Lemma
74
.

In Situation
72
and Situation
60
, the cancellation
R^{q}\Psi_{K/k}(\mathbb{Z}/n(r))=0
for
q>1
follows from Lang’s theorem for complete discrete valuation fields with separably closed residue field. In Situation
63
we use the same methods as for
q=1
to conclude for
q>1
.

Consider Situation
76
.
Then
R^{q}\Psi_{K/k}(\mathbb{Z}/n(r))
is identified with the Galois module
H^{q}(K^{\mathrm{ur}},\mathbb{Z}/n(r))
where
K^{\mathrm{ur}}=\bigcup_{k^{\mathrm{sep}}/l/k\text{ finite}}h^{K/k}(l)
is the maximal unramified extension of
K
(see also Theorem
76
.4 below). By
[
12
]
(Prop. 3.5.2 and 3.5.3), we have
H^{q}(K^{\mathrm{ur}},\mathbb{Z}/n(r))=H^{q}(\hat{K}^{\mathrm{ur}},\mathbb{Z}/n(r))
. We are thus reduced to the cohomology of a complete discrete valuation field with separably closed residue field. Cancellation for
q>1
comes from such a field having
\ell
-cohomological dimension
1
for
\ell\neq\characteristic k
prime, by Lang’s theorem. We have a split exact sequence given by the valuation map :

0\to(\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{K}^{\mathrm{ur}})^{\times}\to(\hat{K}^{\mathrm{ur}})^{\times}\to\mathbb{Z}\to 0.

By passing to
n
-torsion and quotient by
n
-powers, we get
\mu_{n}(\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{K}^{\mathrm{ur}})=\mu_{n}(\hat{K}^{\mathrm{ur}})
and an exact sequence :

0\to(\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{K}^{\mathrm{ur}})^{\times}/n\to(\hat{K}^{\mathrm{ur}})^{\times}\to\mathbb{Z}/n\to 0.

By Hensel’s lemma,
\mu_{n}(\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{K}^{\mathrm{ur}})=\mu_{n}(k^{\mathrm{sep}})
which gives case
q=0
,
r=1
. Hensel’s lemma also gives
(\hat{\mathcal{O}}_{K}^{\mathrm{ur}})^{\times}/n=0
, so
(\hat{K}^{\mathrm{ur}})^{\times}/n=\mathbb{Z}/n
. By Hilbert’s theorem 90 we have
H^{1}(\hat{K}^{\mathrm{ur}},\mathbb{G}_{m})=0
, so by Kummer theory
H^{1}(\hat{K}^{\mathrm{ur}},\mathbb{Z}/n(1))=(\hat{K}^{\mathrm{ur}})^{\times}/n
, which gives the case
q=1
,
r=1
. This concludes in Situation
76
.

Consider Situation
60
and Situation
63
.
We shall adapt the above proof. In Situation
63
we write
S_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}=\mathcal{O}_{K,\mathrm{RPS}}
(Definition
7
) and
R\Psi_{\mathcal{O}_{K}/k}=i^{*}
. In Situation
60
we write
S_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}=\mathcal{O}_{K,\mathrm{RP}}
(Definition
1
) and
R\Psi_{\mathcal{O}_{K}/k}
the pushforward of the premorphism of sites
T\in S_{k}\mapsto h^{\mathcal{O}_{K}/k}(T)
. This way we have a functor
R\Psi_{\mathcal{O}_{K}/k}:D(S_{\mathcal{O}_{K}})\to D(S_{k})
such that for
G\in\Sh(S_{\mathcal{O}_{K}})
and
q\in\mathbb{Z}
, (the restriction to affines of)
R^{q}\Psi_{\mathcal{O}_{K}/k}(G)
is the sheafification of
T\mapsto H^{q}(h^{\mathcal{O}_{K}/k}(T),G)
, either by
[
21
]
(Prop. 3.2) or Lemma
53
.

For any affine
\Spec R\in S_{k}
,
R
is a finite product of integral domains, by definition of
k_{\mathrm{et}}^{\mathrm{perar}}
in Situation
60
or by Lemma
94
.3 in Situation
63
. Thus Proposition
95
.2 yields an exact sequence in
\Sh(S_{k})
:

0\to\Psi_{\mathcal{O}_{K}/k}(\mathbb{G}_{m,\mathcal{O}_{K}})\to\Psi_{K/k}(\mathbb{G}_{m,K})\to\mathbb{Z}\to 0

Passing to
n
-torsion and quotient by
n
-powers, because
\Psi_{K/k}
and
\Psi_{\mathcal{O}_{K}/k}
are left exact, we have an isomorphism
\Psi_{K/k}(\mu_{n})=\Psi_{\mathcal{O}_{K}/k}(\mu_{n})
and an exact sequence :

\Psi_{\mathcal{O}_{K}/k}(\mathbb{G}_{m,\mathcal{O}_{K}})/n\to\Psi_{K/k}(\mathbb{G}_{m,K})/n\to\mathbb{Z}/n\to 0.

For any affine
\Spec R\in S_{k}
,
(h^{\mathcal{O}_{K}/k}(R),\mathfrak{m}_{K}h^{\mathcal{O}_{K}/k}(R))
is a Henselian pair by Proposition
95
.1, thus
\mu_{n}(h^{\mathcal{O}_{K}/k}(R))=\mu_{n}(R)
. After sheafifying we get
\Psi_{\mathcal{O}_{K}/k}(\mu_{n})=\mu_{n}
, which proves case
q=0
,
r=1
.

The map
\Psi_{\mathcal{O}_{K}/k}(\mathbb{G}_{m})\xrightarrow{n}\Psi_{\mathcal{O}_{K}/k}(\mathbb{G}_{m})
is surjective in the étale topology : if
R\in S_{k}
is affine and
x\in h^{\mathcal{O}_{K}/k}(R)^{\times}
, then
\overline{x}\in R
is a unit so
R\to R[x^{1/n}]
is an étale faithfully flat
k
-algebra map and the restriction of
x
to
h^{\mathcal{O}_{K}/k}(R[\overline{x}^{1/n}])
is an
n
-th power because
(h^{\mathcal{O}_{K}/k}(R),\mathfrak{m}_{K}h^{\mathcal{O}_{K}/k}(R))
is a Henselian pair. Thus
\Psi_{K/k}(\mathbb{G}_{m})/n=\mathbb{Z}/n
and Kummer theory gives an injection
\delta:\Psi_{K/k}(\mathbb{G}_{m,K})/n\to R^{1}\Psi_{K/k}(\mu_{n})
. It remains to show
\delta
is surjective and
R^{q}\Psi_{K/k}(\mathbb{Z}/n(r))=0
for
q\geq 2
, which we check at stalks.

In Situation
61
, the strict Henselization at a prime of an object of
S_{k}=k_{\mathrm{et}}^{\mathrm{perar}}
is the algebraic closure of a perfect field extension of
k
. Let
F
be such a field extension with algebraic closure
\overline{F}
. Then :

R^{q}\Psi_{K/k}(G)_{\overline{F}}=\varinjlim_{E/F\text{ finite}}H^{q}(h^{K/k}(E),G)=H^{q}(\varinjlim_{E/F\text{ finite}}h^{K/k}(E),G)=H^{q}(h^{K/k}(\overline{F}),G)

where the last equality is by
[
3
]
(Cor. 1.13.2) :
h^{K/k}(F)
is a discrete valuation field (Proposition
95
.2), with maximal unramified extension
\varinjlim_{E/F}h^{K/k}(E)
a Henselian discrete valuation field, whose completion is precisely
h^{K/k}(\overline{F})
. Since
h^{K/k}(\overline{F})
is a complete discrete valuation field with algebraically closed residue field,, we get
R^{1}\Psi_{K/k}(\mathbb{G}_{m})(\overline{F})=0
by Hilbert’s theorem 90, so
\delta
is surjective, and
R^{q}\Psi_{K/k}(\mathbb{Z}/n(r))(\overline{F})=0
for
q\geq 2
by Lang’s theorem. This proves the required properties in Situation
61
.

In Situation
63
, consider
R\in S_{k}
a relatively perfectly smooth algebra and
R^{\prime}
the strict Henselization of
R
at a prime identified with a point
x\in\Spec R
. By
[
21
]
(Prop. 4.1 and 4.2), there exists an
\mathcal{O}_{K}
-algebra
B
with the following properties.

1.

B\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}k=R^{\prime}
;

2.

B
is a filtered colimit
\varinjlim_{\alpha}B_{\alpha}
of smooth
\mathcal{O}_{K}
-algebras
B_{\alpha}
;

3.

(B,\mathfrak{m}_{K}B)
is a Henselian pair  ;

4.

We have isomorphisms
R^{q}\Psi_{K/k}(G)_{\overline{x}}=H^{q}(B_{K},G)=H^{q}(h^{K/k}(R),G)
induced by restrictions, for
G\in\Sh_{\mathrm{fin}}(S_{K})
and
q\in\mathbb{Z}
, where
B_{K}=B\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}K
.

By (1.), (3.), Proposition
95
.1 and
[
34
]
(Th. 09ZI) we have for all
q\geq 0
:

H^{q}(h^{\mathcal{O}_{K}/k}(R^{\prime}),\mathbb{Z}/n(1))=H^{q}(B,\mathbb{Z}/n(1))=H^{q}(R^{\prime},\mathbb{Z}/n(1)).

Write
Z=\Spec R^{\prime}
,
X=\Spec h^{\mathcal{O}_{K}/k}(R^{\prime})
,
U=\Spec h^{K/k}(R^{\prime})
,
X^{\prime}=\Spec B
and
U^{\prime}=\Spec B_{K}
. By (4.) and the above, restriction by
X\to X^{\prime}
gives an isomorphism of the exact sequences of
[
25
]
(Prop. III.1.25) :

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{q}_{Z}(X,\mathbb{Z}/n(1))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{q}(X,\mathbb{Z}/n(1))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{q}(U,\mathbb{Z}/n(1))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{q+1}_{Z}(X,\mathbb{Z}/n(1))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
