{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{q}_{Z}(X^{\prime},\mathbb{Z}/n(1))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{q}(X^{\prime},\mathbb{Z}/n(1))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{q}(U^{\prime},\mathbb{Z}/n(1))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{q+1}_{Z}(X^{\prime},\mathbb{Z}/n(1))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cdots}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

Writing
\overline{F}
the residue field of
R^{\prime}
, by
[
34
]
(Th. 09ZI) we have
H^{q}(X^{\prime},\mathbb{Z}/n(1))=H^{q}(\overline{F},\mathbb{Z}/n(1))=0
for
q\geq 1
because
\overline{F}
is separably closed. Thus
H^{q}(U,\mathbb{Z}/n(1))=H^{q}(U^{\prime},\mathbb{Z}/n(1))=H^{q+1}_{Z}(X^{\prime},\mathbb{Z}/n(1))
for
q\geq 1
. By Lemma
74
.2 applied to the codimension
1
closed immersion of regular schemes
\Spec k\to\Spec\mathcal{O}_{K}
and the ind-smooth
\mathcal{O}_{K}
-scheme
X^{\prime}
we have
H^{q+1}_{Z}(X^{\prime},\mathbb{Z}/n(1))=H^{q-1}(R^{\prime},\mathbb{Z}/n)
for
q\in\mathbb{Z}
. Thus
R^{q}\Psi_{K/k}(\mathbb{Z}/n(1))_{\overline{x}}=H^{q-1}(R^{\prime},\mathbb{Z}/n)=H^{q-1}(\overline{F},\mathbb{Z}/n)
for
q\geq 1
.

This shows
\delta:\mathbb{Z}/n\to R^{1}\Psi_{K/k}(\mathbb{Z}/n(1))
is bijective, because it is injective and at each stalk is an injection of finite groups of equal orders
n
(we do not claim that the isomorphism
R^{q}\Psi_{K/k}(\mathbb{Z}/n(1))_{\overline{x}}=\mathbb{Z}/n
of purity is induced by
\delta
). Also
R^{q}\Psi_{K/k}(\mathbb{Z}/n(1))=0
for
q>1
, which concludes for Situation
63
.
∎

Theorem 76
.

Consider

1.

The functor

. If

.

in
D(S_{k})
.

3.

For any

with Cartier dual

, we have a perfect pairing :

R\Psi_{K/k}(G)\otimes^{L}R\Psi_{K/k}(G^{\vee})\to R\Psi_{K/k}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}/\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(d+1))\xrightarrow{\mathrm{tr}}\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}/\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(d)[-1]

which gives perfect Cartier dualities of finite étale
\ell
-groups for

:

4.

For
G\in\Sh(S_{K})
and

,

is the sheafification of
T\in S_{k}\mapsto H^{q}(h^{K/k}(T),G)
. In particular
R\Psi_{K/k}(G)_{k^{\mathrm{sep}}}=R\Gamma(K^{\mathrm{ur}},G_{K^{\mathrm{sep}}})
, where
K^{\mathrm{ur}}=\bigcup_{l/k\text{ finite}}h^{K/k}(l)
is the maximal unramified extension of
K
. If in addition
G
is torsion then
R\Psi_{K/k}(G)_{k^{\mathrm{sep}}}=R\Gamma(\hat{K}^{\mathrm{ur}},G_{K^{\mathrm{sep}}})

.

. By exactness of

R\Gamma(K^{\prime},G)\otimes R\Gamma(K^{\prime},G^{\vee})\to R\Gamma(K^{\prime},\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}/\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(d))\xrightarrow{\mathrm{tr}}\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}/\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(d-1)[-1]

First consider the case

, for some

as a Galois module over

. By Shapiro’s lemma and Lemma

we have a diagram of pairings :

where the top and middle horizontal arrows are given by the cup-product, and the bottom arrow, given by multiplication, is a perfect pairing. The top half of the diagram commutes by the relation
x\cup\mathrm{cores}(y)=\mathrm{cores}(\mathrm{res}(x)\cup y)
for cup products in group cohomology. The commutativity of the bottom half reduces to

Hence
H^{q}(K^{\prime},G)\otimes H^{1-q}(K^{\prime},G^{\vee})\to H^{1}(K^{\prime},\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}/\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(d+1))
is isomorphic to a perfect pairing, thus perfect.

Now consider
G\in\Sh_{\ell}(K^{\prime}_{\mathrm{et}})
general. There is a finite Galois extension
L/K^{\prime}
over which
G
is trivial. Write
P=\mathrm{Res}_{L/K^{\prime}}(G|_{L})
. Then we have a short exact sequence, for some
Q\in\Sh_{\ell}(K_{\mathrm{et}})
:

0\to G\to P\to Q\to 0

which gives diagrams with exact rows, commutative up to signs :

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{0}(K^{\prime},G)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{0}(K^{\prime},P)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{0}(K^{\prime},Q)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{1}(K^{\prime},G^{\vee}_{K})^{\vee}_{k}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{1}(K^{\prime},P^{\vee}_{K})^{\vee}_{k}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{1}(K^{\prime},Q^{\vee}_{K})^{\vee}_{k}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{G}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{P}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varphi_{Q}}

where
(-)^{\vee}_{K}
and
(-)^{\vee}_{k}
denote Cartier duality over
K_{\mathrm{et}}
and
k_{\mathrm{et}}
respectively. We have seen that
\varphi_{P}
is an isomorphism. The map
\varphi_{P}
is injective, so
\varphi_{G}
is injective. This applies to any
G\in\Sh_{\ell}(k_{\mathrm{et}})
, so
\varphi_{Q}
is also injective. By the four lemma
\varphi_{G}
is surjective, thus an isomorphism as desired.
∎

4.4
Higher local fields

We now compose the previous steps to get results over higher local fields.

Definition 77
.

For
d\geq 0
, a
d
-local field
is a field
K
equipped with a sequence of fields
K=k_{d},\dots,k_{0}
where
k_{0}
is perfect and for all
i>0
,
k_{i}
is a Henselian discrete valuation field with residue field
k_{i-1}
, and
k_{i}
is also complete whenever
\characteristic k_{i}>0
. The field
k_{i}
is referred to as the
i
-local residue field of
K
. We say
K
has
totally equal characteristic
p
if
\characteristic k_{d}=\characteristic k_{0}=p
, or
mixed characteristic
(0,p)
at level
s
for
1\leq s\leq d
if
\characteristic k_{d}=\characteristic k_{s}=0
and
\characteristic k_{s-1}=p>0
.

If
K
is Henselian discrete valuation and
L/K
is finite then the absolute value of
K
extends in a unique way to
L
. Thus if
L=l_{d}
is a finite extension of a
d
-local field
K=k_{d}
, then by induction on
d
one can show that
l_{d}
is again
d
-local, and one can take its
i
-local residue field
l_{i}
to be a finite extension of
k_{i}
for all
0\leq i\leq d
. There exist
2
2

2

This is true more generally if
K=k_{d},\dots,k_{0}
is such that
k_{i}
is Henselian discrete valuation with residue field
k_{i-1}
and
\hat{k}_{i}/k_{i}
is separable for each
i>0
, or equivalently
\mathcal{O}_{k_{i}}
is excellent : see
[
32
]
(§I.4, Prop. 10
integers
e_{1},\dots,e_{d}\geq 1
such that the valuation
v_{l_{i}}
restricts to
e_{i}v_{k_{i}}
on
k_{i}
, called the ramification indices of
L/K
, which satisfy
e_{d}\cdots e_{1}[l_{0}:k_{0}]=[L:K]
. We say
L/K
is
totally unramified
if each
l_{i}/k_{i}
is separable and
e_{1}=\dots=e_{d}=1
. The union of all totally unramified finite extensions of
K
in a fixed separable closure is called the
maximal totally unramified extension
of
K
, and written
K^{\mathrm{tur}}
.

If
\characteristic k_{d-1}=\characteristic k_{0}=p>0
then for any relatively perfect
k_{0}
-algebra
R
we write :

h^{\hat{K}/k_{0}}(R)=h^{\hat{k}_{d}/k_{d-1}}(h^{k_{d-1}/k_{d-2}}(\dots h^{k_{1}/k_{0}}(R)\dots))

the iterated Kato lift of
R
over the successive
k_{i}
’s (note that by assumption, only
K=k_{d}
is possibly not complete for its discrete valuation, so we must complete it). In general if
R
is an étale
k_{0}
-algebra, we write :

h^{K/k_{0}}(R)=h^{k_{d}/k_{d-1}}(h^{k_{d-1}/k_{d-2}}(\dots h^{k_{1}/k_{0}}(R)\dots))

the iterated Henselian lift of
R
to an étale algebra over
K
. We will also write
h^{\hat{K}/k_{0}}(T)=\Spec h^{\hat{K}/k_{0}}(R)
(resp.
h^{K/k_{0}}(T)=\Spec h^{K/k_{0}}(R)
) if
T=\Spec R
. Note that
K^{\mathrm{tur}}
is exactly the union of the
h^{K/k_{0}}(l)
for
l
ranging over finite extensions of
k_{0}
.

Situation 78
.

Let
K=k_{d},\dots,k_{0}
be a
d
-local field. Assume
d\geq 2
and
K
has mixed characteristic
(0,p)
at level
2
. Consider
X
a proper, smooth, geometrically integral
K
-variety of dimension
e\geq 0
and
U\subseteq X
a nonempty open. We define functors
R\Psi_{U/k_{0}},R\Psi_{U/k_{0},c}:D(U_{\mathrm{et}})\to D(k_{0,\mathrm{et}}^{\mathrm{perar}})
by :

\displaystyle R\Psi_{U/k_{0}}

\displaystyle=R\Psi_{k_{1}/k_{0}}^{p}\circ Lv^{*}\circ R\Psi_{k_{2}/k_{1}}^{m}\circ u^{*}\circ R\Psi_{k_{3}/k_{2}}^{0}\circ\cdots\circ R\Psi_{k_{d}/k_{d-1}}^{0}\circ R\Psi_{U/K}^{v},

\displaystyle R\Psi_{U/k_{0},c}

\displaystyle=R\Psi_{k_{1}/k_{0}}^{p}\circ Lv^{*}\circ R\Psi_{k_{2}/k_{1}}^{m}\circ u^{*}\circ R\Psi_{k_{3}/k_{2}}^{0}\circ\cdots\circ R\Psi_{k_{d}/k_{d-1}}^{0}\circ R\Psi_{U/K,c}^{v},

where
u:k_{2,\mathrm{Et}}\to k_{2,\mathrm{et}}
and
v:k_{1,\mathrm{RP}}\to k_{1,\mathrm{RPS}}
are the premorphisms of sites defined by identity.

Theorem 79
.

Consider Situation
78
.

1.

The functors
R\Psi_{U/k_{0}}
and
R\Psi_{U/k_{0},c}
send
D_{p}(U_{\mathrm{et}})
to
D^{b}_{\mathcal{W}_{k_{0}}}(k_{0,\mathrm{et}}^{\mathrm{perar}})
, and
D_{\ell}(U_{\mathrm{et}})
to
D_{\ell}(k_{0,\mathrm{et}}^{\mathrm{perar}})
for
\ell\neq p
prime. If
G\in\Sh_{\mathrm{fin}}(U_{\mathrm{et}})
then
R^{q}\Psi_{U/k_{0}}(G)=R^{q}\Psi_{U/k_{0},c}(G)=0
for
q\neq 0,\dots,d+2e
.

2.

There exists a canonical trace morphism
\mathrm{tr}:R\Psi_{U/k_{0},c}(\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z}(d+e))\to\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z}[-d-2e]
in
D(k_{0,\mathrm{et}}^{\mathrm{perar}})
.

3.

For
G\in D_{\mathrm{fin}}(U_{\mathrm{et}})
and
G^{\vee}=R\HOM_{U_{\mathrm{et}}}(G,\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z}(d))
we have a perfect pairing in
D(k_{0,\mathrm{et}}^{\mathrm{perar}})
:

R\Psi_{U/k_{0}}(G)\otimes^{L}R\Psi_{U/k_{0},c}(G^{\vee})\to R\Psi_{U/k_{0},c}(\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z}(d+e))\xrightarrow{\mathrm{tr}}\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z}[-d-2e]

which induces perfect Cartier dualities of finite étale
\ell
-primary
k_{0}
-groups, if
G\in D_{\ell}(U_{\mathrm{et}})
:

R^{q}\Psi_{U/k_{0}}(G)\otimes R^{d+2e-q}\Psi_{U/k_{0},c}(G^{\vee})\to R^{d+2e}\Psi_{U/k_{0},c}(\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}/\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}(d+e))\xrightarrow{\mathrm{tr}}\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}/\mathbb{Z}_{\ell}

or perfect Serre dualities of connected and finite étale parts if
G\in D_{p}(U_{\mathrm{et}})
:

\displaystyle R^{q}\Psi_{U/k_{0}}(G)^{0}

\displaystyle=\EXT^{1}_{k_{0,\mathrm{et}}^{\mathrm{perar}}}(R^{d+2e-1-q}\Psi_{U/k_{0},c}(G^{\vee})^{0},\mathbb{Q}_{p}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}),

\displaystyle\pi_{0}(R^{q}\Psi_{U/k_{0}}(G))

\displaystyle=\HOM_{k_{0,\mathrm{et}}^{\mathrm{perar}}}(\pi_{0}(R^{d+2e-q}\Psi_{U/k_{0},c}(G^{\vee})),\mathbb{Q}_{p}/\mathbb{Z}_{p}).

Proof of Theorem
79
.

The components of the composite functors
R\Psi_{U/k_{0}}
and
R\Psi_{U/k_{0},c}
each satisfy dualities for
p
-torsion coefficients :
R\Psi_{U/K}^{v}
and
R\Psi_{U/K,c}^{v}
by Theorem
71
,
R\Psi_{k_{i}/k_{i-1}}^{0}
for
i>2
by Theorem
76
,
Lv^{*}\circ R\Psi_{k_{2}/k_{0}}^{m}\circ u^{*}
by Theorem
68
and Remark
69
, and
R\Psi_{k_{1}/k_{0}}^{p}
by Theorem
76
. They each satisfy dualities for
\ell
-torsion coefficients by Theorem
71
, Theorem
76
, Lemma
56
and Theorem
57
.4. A perfect Cartier (resp. Serre) duality of objects of
D_{\ell}(k_{0,\mathrm{et}}^{\mathrm{perar}})
(resp.
D^{b}_{\mathcal{W}_{k_{0}}}(k_{0,\mathrm{et}}^{\mathrm{perar}})
) decomposes automatically as in
(3.)
by Theorem
57
.1 (resp. Theorem
43
.5).

The theorem follows, except we only have
R^{q}\Psi_{U/k_{0}}(G)=R^{q}\Psi_{U/k_{0},c}(G)=0
for
q\neq 0,\dots,d+2e+1
if
G\in\Sh_{\mathrm{fin}}(U_{\mathrm{et}})
has
p
-torsion. This is because the sheaf
R\Psi_{k_{2}/k_{0}}(G)=R\Psi_{k_{1}/k_{0}}^{p}\circ Lv^{*}\circ R\Psi_{k_{2}/k_{1}}^{m}\circ u^{*}(G)
is
a priori
concentrated in degrees
0,1,2,3
and not
0,1,2
. We show
R^{3}\Psi_{k_{2}/k_{0}}(G)=0
also for
G\in\Sh_{p}(k_{2,\mathrm{et}})
.

Let
R\in k_{0,\mathrm{et}}^{\mathrm{perar}}
and take
\overline{x}\to\Spec R
a geometric point. In Proposition
83
below we will see that :

R^{3}\Psi_{k_{2}/k_{0}}(G)_{\overline{x}}=\varinjlim_{R^{\prime}/R}H^{3}(h^{\hat{k}_{2}/k_{0}}(R^{\prime}),G)

where
R^{\prime}
runs over étale neighborhoods of
\overline{x}
. Since
h^{\hat{k}_{2}/k_{0}}
commutes with finite products, we can assume
R
is a perfect field,
\overline{x}
is given by an algebraic closure
\overline{R}
, and
R^{\prime}
runs over finite field extensions of
R
. Then :

R^{3}\Psi_{k_{2}/k_{0}}(G)_{\overline{x}}=H^{3}(h^{\hat{k}_{2}/k_{0}}(R)^{\mathrm{tur}},G)

where
M_{2}=h^{\hat{k}_{2}/k_{0}}(R)^{\mathrm{tur}}
is a Henselian discrete valuation field with residue field
M_{1}=h^{k_{1}/k_{0}}(R)^{\mathrm{ur}}
, itself Henselian discrete valuation with algebraically closed residue field
M_{0}=R^{\mathrm{sep}}
. Clearly
M_{0}
has cohomological dimension
0
; then inductively
M_{i}
has cohomological dimension at most
\cd(\hat{M}_{i})=\cd(M_{i-1})+1\leq i
by
[
3
]
(Cor. 1.18.2) and
[
18
]
(Cor. of Th. 3). Thus
H^{3}(M_{2},G)=0
, and
R^{3}\Psi_{k_{2}/k_{0}}(G)_{\overline{x}}=0
as desired.
∎

Remark 80
.

Since the composite
\alpha_{k_{0}}R\Psi_{k_{1}/k_{0}}^{p}:D(k_{1,\mathrm{RP}})\to D(k_{0,\mathrm{proet}}^{\mathrm{indrat}})
also satisfies a duality by Remark
62
, where
\alpha_{k_{0}}:D(k_{0,\mathrm{et}}^{\mathrm{perar}})\to D(k_{0,\mathrm{proet}}^{\mathrm{indrat}})
is the change of site functor, Theorem
79
also holds if we replace
k_{0,\mathrm{et}}^{\mathrm{perar}}
by
k_{0,\mathrm{proet}}^{\mathrm{indrat}}
and
R\Psi_{U/k_{0}}
and
R\Psi_{U/k_{0},c}
by their composites with
\alpha_{k_{0}}
.

Theorem 81
.

Consider Situation
78
. Assume
k_{0}
is finite. Define functors
D(U_{\mathrm{et}})\to D(*_{\mathrm{proet}})
:

R\Psi_{U/*}=R\Psi_{k_{0}/*}\circ\alpha_{k_{0}}\circ R\Psi_{U/k_{0}},\qquad R\Psi_{U/*,c}=R\Psi_{k_{0}/*}\circ\alpha_{k_{0}}\circ R\Psi_{U/k_{0},c}

where
R\Psi_{k_{0},*}:D(k_{0,\mathrm{proet}}^{\mathrm{indrat}})\to D(*_{\mathrm{proet}})
is the functor of Situation
52
.

1.

The functors
R\Psi_{U/*}
and
R\Psi_{U/*,c}
send
D_{p}(U_{\mathrm{et}})
to
D^{b}_{\mathcal{W}_{p}}(*_{\mathrm{proet}})
, and
D_{\ell}(U_{\mathrm{et}})
to
D_{\ell}(*_{\mathrm{proet}})
for
\ell\neq p
prime. If
G\in\Sh_{\mathrm{fin}}(U_{\mathrm{et}})
then
R^{q}\Psi_{U/*}(G)=R^{q}\Psi_{U/*,c}(G)=0
for
q\neq 0,\dots,d+2e+1
. In particular
R^{q}\Psi_{U/*}(G)
and
R^{q}\Psi_{U/*,c}(G)
are representable by locally compact Hausdorff groups for
q\in\mathbb{Z}
.

2.

There exists a canonical trace morphism
\mathrm{tr}:R\Psi_{U/*,c}(\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z}(d+e))\to\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z}[-d-2e-1]
in
D(*_{\mathrm{proet}})
.

3.

For
G\in D_{\mathrm{fin}}(U_{\mathrm{et}})
and
G^{\vee}=R\HOM_{U_{\mathrm{et}}}(G,\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z}(d))
we have a perfect pairing in
D(*_{\mathrm{proet}})
:

R\Psi_{U/*}(G)\otimes^{L}R\Psi_{U/*,c}(G^{\vee})\to R\Psi_{U/*,c}(\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z}(d+e))\xrightarrow{\mathrm{tr}}\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z}[-d-2e-1]

which induces perfect Pontryagin dualities of locally compact Hausdorff groups for
q\in\mathbb{Z}
:

R^{q}\Psi_{U/*}(G)\otimes R^{d+2e+1-q}R\Psi_{U/*,c}(G^{\vee})\to R^{d+2e+1}R\Psi_{U/*,c}(\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z}(d+e))\xrightarrow{\mathrm{tr}}\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z}.

Proof of Theorem
81
.

This follows from Theorem
79
, Remark
80
and Proposition
54
for
p
-primary coefficients. For
G\in\Sh_{\ell}(k_{\mathrm{proet}}^{\mathrm{indrat}})
and
q\in\mathbb{Z}
,
R^{q}\Psi_{k_{0}/*}(G)
is the sheaf representable by the finite group
H^{q}(k_{\mathrm{et}},G)
. We have
H^{q}(k_{\mathrm{et}},G)=0
if
q\neq 0,1
,
H^{1}(k_{\mathrm{et}},\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})=\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z}
, and perfect pairings :

H^{q}(k_{\mathrm{et}},G)\otimes H^{1-q}(k_{\mathrm{et}},G^{\vee})\to H^{1}(k_{\mathrm{et}},\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})=\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z}

for all
q\in\mathbb{Z}
where
G^{\vee}=\HOM_{k_{0,\mathrm{proet}}^{\mathrm{indrat}}}(G,\mathbb{Q}_{\ell}/\mathbb{Z}_{\ell})
is the Cartier dual of
G
. The corresponding properties for
R\Psi_{k_{0},*}
follow, and so does the theorem from composing with Theorem
79
.
∎

It remains to give arithmetic interpretations of Theorem
79
and Theorem
81
.

Lemma 82
.

Let
v:S^{\prime}\to S
be a premorphism of sites.

1.

For
F,F^{\prime}\in D(S)
, there is a canonical morphism
Lv^{*}(F\otimes^{L}F^{\prime})\to Lv^{*}F\otimes^{L}Lv^{*}F^{\prime}
in
D(S^{\prime})
. If
S^{\prime}
has finite products and
v^{-1}:S^{\prime}\to S
preserves those products, then this is an isomorphism.

2.

For
G,G^{\prime}\in D(S^{\prime})
the cup-product
Rv_{*}G\otimes^{L}Rv_{*}G^{\prime}\to Rv_{*}(G\otimes^{L}G^{\prime})
corresponds under the adjunction of
Lv^{*}
and
Rv_{*}
to the composite :

Lv^{*}(Rv_{*}G\otimes^{L}Rv_{*}G^{\prime})\xrightarrow{\text{{(1.)}}}(Lv^{*}Rv_{*}G)\otimes^{L}(Lv^{*}Rv_{*}G^{\prime})\xrightarrow{\varepsilon_{G}\otimes\varepsilon_{G^{\prime}}}G\otimes^{L}G^{\prime}

where
\varepsilon:Lv^{*}Rv_{*}\to\mathrm{id}
is the counit in
D(S^{\prime})
.

Proof of Lemma
82
.

1.
The cup-product of
Rv_{*}
and the unit
F^{\prime}\to Rv_{*}Lv^{*}F^{\prime}
define a natural map
Rv_{*}R\HOM_{S^{\prime}}(Lv^{*}F^{\prime},T)\to R\HOM_{S}(F^{\prime},Rv_{*}T)
for
T\in D(S^{\prime})
. By adjunctions, we get a morphism :

\displaystyle\Hom_{D(S^{\prime})}((Lv^{*}F)\otimes^{L}(Lv^{*}F^{\prime}),T)

\displaystyle=\Hom_{D(S)}(F,Rv_{*}R\HOM_{D(S^{\prime})}(Lv^{*}F^{\prime},T))

\displaystyle\to\Hom_{D(S)}(F,R\HOM_{D(S)}(F^{\prime},Rv_{*}T))

\displaystyle=\Hom_{D(S^{\prime})}(Lv^{*}(F\otimes^{L}F^{\prime}),T).

hence a canonical morphism
Lv^{*}(F\otimes^{L}F^{\prime})\to(Lv^{*}F)\otimes^{L}(Lv^{*}F^{\prime})
by Yoneda’s lemma. This is an isomorphism whenever the map
Rv_{*}R\HOM_{S^{\prime}}(Lv^{*}F^{\prime},T)\to R\HOM_{S}(F^{\prime},Rv_{*}T)
is an isomorphism, which is the case under the given additional assumption by
[
35
]
(Prop. 3.1).

2.
Working out the definition of the morphism of
(1.)
for
F,F^{\prime}\in D(S)
reveals it corresponds, under the adjunction of
Lv^{*}
and
Rv_{*}
, to the composite :

F\otimes^{L}F^{\prime}\xrightarrow{\eta_{F}\otimes^{L}\eta_{F^{\prime}}}(Rv_{*}Lv^{*}F)\otimes^{L}(Rv_{*}Lv^{*}F^{\prime}))\xrightarrow{\cup_{Lv^{*}F,Lv^{*}F^{\prime}}}Rv_{*}((Lv^{*}F)\otimes^{L}(Lv^{*}F^{\prime}))

where
\cup_{G,G^{\prime}}:Rv_{*}G\otimes^{L}Rv_{*}G^{\prime}\to Rv_{*}(G\otimes^{L}G^{\prime})
is the cup-product in
D(S)
for
G,G^{\prime}\in D(S^{\prime})
, and
\eta_{F}:F\to Rv_{*}Lv^{*}F
is the unit in
D(S)
. Thus we want a commutative square, for
G,G^{\prime}\in D(S^{\prime})
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Rv_{*}G\otimes^{L}Rv_{*}G^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(Rv_{*}Lv^{*}Rv_{*}G)\otimes^{L}(Rv_{*}Lv^{*}Rv_{*}G^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Rv_{*}(Lv^{*}Rv_{*}G\otimes^{L}Lv^{*}Rv_{*}G^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Rv_{*}G\otimes^{L}Rv_{*}G^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Rv_{*}G\otimes^{L}Rv_{*}G^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Rv_{*}(G\otimes^{L}G)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\eta\otimes\eta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cup}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Rv_{*}\varepsilon\otimes^{L}Rv_{*}\varepsilon}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Rv_{*}(\varepsilon\otimes^{L}\varepsilon)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cup_{G,G^{\prime}}}

where
\varepsilon:Lv^{*}Rv_{*}\to\mathrm{id}
is the counit. The left-hand side commutes by unit-counit relations. The commutativity of the square :

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(Rv_{*}Lv^{*}Rv_{*}G)\otimes^{L}(Rv_{*}Lv^{*}Rv_{*}G^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Rv_{*}(Lv^{*}Rv_{*}G\otimes^{L}Lv^{*}Rv_{*}G^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Rv_{*}G\otimes^{L}Rv_{*}G^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Rv_{*}(G\otimes^{L}G)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Rv_{*}\varepsilon_{G}\otimes^{L}Rv_{*}\varepsilon_{G^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cup_{Lv^{*}Rv_{*}G,Lv^{*}Rv_{*}G^{\prime}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Rv_{*}(\varepsilon_{G}\otimes^{L}\varepsilon_{G^{\prime}})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cup_{G,G^{\prime}}}

follows from the naturality of the cup-product
\cup_{G,G^{\prime}}
in
G
and
G^{\prime}
, clear by definition (
[
37
]
Prop. 2.4).
∎

Proposition 83
.

Consider Situation
78
.

1.

Assume
d=2
and
X=U=\Spec K
. Let
R\tilde{\Psi}_{K/k_{0}}:D(K_{\mathrm{Et}})\to D(k_{0,\mathrm{et}}^{\mathrm{perar}})
be the derived pushforward of the premorphism
T/k_{0}\mapsto h^{\hat{K}/k_{0}}(T)
. Then for
G\in D_{\mathrm{fin}}(K_{\mathrm{et}})
we have :

R\Psi_{K/k_{0}}(G)=R\tilde{\Psi}_{K/k_{0}}\circ u^{*}(G).

In particular,
R^{q}\Psi_{K/k_{0}}(G)
is the sheafification of
T\in k_{0,\mathrm{et}}^{\mathrm{perar}}\mapsto H^{q}(h^{K/k_{0}}(T),G)
for
q\in\mathbb{Z}
.

2.

In the situation of
(1.)
, we also have a commutative diagram of pairings for all
q,s\in\mathbb{Z}
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Psi_{K/k_{0}}(G)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\otimes^{L}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Psi_{K/k_{0}}(G^{\vee})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Psi_{K/k_{0}}(\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z}(2))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u^{*}R\tilde{\Psi}_{K/k_{0}}(G)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\otimes^{L}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Lu^{*}R\tilde{\Psi}_{K/k_{0}}(G^{\vee})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u^{*}R\tilde{\Psi}_{K/k_{0}}(\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z}(2))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

where
G^{\vee}=R\HOM_{K_{\mathrm{et}}}(G,\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z}(2))
, the upper pairing is as in Theorem
79
and the lower pairing is given by monoidality of
u^{*}
and the cup-product for the derived pushforward
R\tilde{\Psi}_{K/k_{0}}
.

3.

Consider the general case. Let
R\Psi^{\mathrm{et}}_{U/k_{0}}:D(U_{\mathrm{et}})\to D(k_{0,\mathrm{et}})
be the derived pushforward of the premorphism
T/k_{0}\mapsto U_{h^{K/k_{0}}(T)}
. Let
R\Psi^{\mathrm{et}}_{U/k_{0},c}=R\Psi^{\mathrm{et}}_{X/k_{0}}\circ j_{!}
. Finally let
\beta_{k_{0}}:k_{0,\mathrm{et}}^{\mathrm{perar}}\to k_{0,\mathrm{et}}
be the premorphism of sites defined by identity. Then for
G\in D_{\mathrm{fin}}(K_{\mathrm{et}})
we have :

\beta_{k_{0},*}\circ R\Psi_{U/k_{0}}(G)=R\Psi^{\mathrm{et}}_{U/k_{0}}(G),\qquad\beta_{k_{0},*}\circ R\Psi_{U/k_{0},c}(G)=R\Psi^{\mathrm{et}}_{U/k_{0},c}(G).

In particular we have identifications in
\Sh(k_{0,\mathrm{et}})
, for
q\in\mathbb{Z}
:

\displaystyle R^{q}\Psi_{U/k_{0}}(G)_{k^{\mathrm{sep}}}

\displaystyle=H^{q}(U_{K^{\mathrm{tur}}},G|_{U_{K^{\mathrm{tur}}}})

\displaystyle R^{q}\Psi_{U/k_{0},c}(G)_{k^{\mathrm{sep}}}

\displaystyle=H^{q}_{c}(U_{K^{\mathrm{tur}}},G|_{U_{K^{\mathrm{tur}}}})=H^{q}(X_{K^{\mathrm{tur}}},j_{K^{\mathrm{tur}},!}(G|_{U_{K^{\mathrm{tur}}}})).

4.

In the situation of
(3.)
, we also have a commutative diagram of pairings for all
q,s\in\mathbb{Z}
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R^{q}\Psi_{U/k_{0}}(G)_{k^{\mathrm{sep}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\otimes^{L}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R^{s}\Psi_{U/k_{0},c}(G)_{k^{\mathrm{sep}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R^{q+s}\Psi_{U/k_{0},c}(\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z}(d+e))_{k^{\mathrm{sep}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{q}(U_{K^{\mathrm{tur}}},G|_{U_{K^{\mathrm{tur}}}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\otimes^{L}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{s}_{c}(U_{K^{\mathrm{tur}}},G|_{U_{K^{\mathrm{tur}}}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{q+s}_{c}(U_{K^{\mathrm{tur}}},\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z}(d+e))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

where
G^{\vee}=R\HOM_{U_{\mathrm{et}}}(G,\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z}(d+e))
, and the pairings are of Theorem
79
and the lower pairing is the cup product of étale cohomology.

Proof of Proposition
83
.

1.
Since
u^{*}
defines an equivalence
D_{\mathrm{fin}}(K_{\mathrm{et}})=D_{\mathrm{fin}}(K_{\mathrm{Et}})
, we can identify
G
with an object of
D_{\mathrm{fin}}(K_{\mathrm{Et}})
and it suffices to prove :

R\Psi_{k_{1}/k_{0}}^{p}\circ Lv^{*}\circ R\Psi_{K/k_{1}}^{m}(G)=R\tilde{\Psi}_{K/k_{0}}(G).

We reproduce an argument shared by Suzuki in a private communication. The idea is to eliminate the change of site step
Lv_{k_{1}}^{*}:D(k_{1,\mathrm{RPS}})\to D(k_{1,\mathrm{RPS}})
by modifying
R\Psi_{K/k_{1}}^{m}
and
R\Psi_{k_{1}/k_{0}}^{p}
to pass through the same site
k_{1,\mathrm{PRPS}}
. Define
k_{1,\mathrm{PRPS}}
as the category of
k_{1}
-schemes which are cofiltered limits of relatively perfectly smooth schemes along affine transition morphisms, with all
k_{1}
-scheme morphisms and the étale topology. Define premorphisms :

k_{1,\mathrm{RP}}\xrightarrow{v_{P}}k_{1,\mathrm{PRPS}}\xrightarrow{w}k_{1,\mathrm{RPS}}

by identity on the underlying categories. Thus
v=w\circ v_{\mathcal{P}}
.

For
F
a sheaf of sets over
k_{1,\mathrm{RPS}}
and
X=\varprojlim_{i}X_{i}\in k_{1,\mathrm{PRPS}}
, we have
w^{*}F(X)=\varinjlim_{i}S(X_{i})
, from which it follows that the pullback of sets for
w
is exact, and
w
is a morphism of sites. Clearly
v_{\mathcal{P},*}
is exact and
v_{\mathcal{P},*}v_{\mathcal{P}}^{*}=\mathrm{id}
: in fact
v_{\mathcal{P},*}F(X)=F(X)
for
F\in\Sh(k_{1,\mathrm{RP}})
and
X\in k_{1,\mathrm{PRPS}}
, and
v_{\mathcal{P}}^{*}F(X)=F(X)
for
F\in\Sh(k_{1,\mathrm{PRPS}})
and
X\in k_{1,\mathrm{PRPS}}
. Hence we also have
v_{\mathcal{P},*}Lv_{\mathcal{P}}^{*}=\mathrm{id}
.

By
[
40
]
(proof of Prop. 5.4), for
T\in k_{0,\mathrm{et}}^{\mathrm{perar}}
, the
k_{1}
-scheme
(\psi_{k_{1}/k_{0}}^{p})^{-1}(T)=h^{k_{1}/k_{0}}(T)
belongs to
k_{1,\mathrm{PRPS}}
. Hence the premorphism
\psi_{k_{1}/k_{0}}:k_{1,\mathrm{RP}}\to k_{0,\mathrm{et}}^{\mathrm{perar}}
factors through
v_{P}
as
\psi_{k_{1}/k_{0},\mathcal{P}}:k_{1,\mathrm{PRPS}}\to k_{0,\mathrm{et}}^{\mathrm{perar}}
and we have :

R\Psi_{k_{1}/k_{0}}^{p}Lv^{*}=R\Psi_{k_{1}/k_{0},\mathcal{P}}^{p}v_{\mathcal{P},*}Lv_{\mathcal{P}}^{*}w^{*}=R\Psi_{k_{1}/k_{0},\mathcal{P}}^{p}w^{*}

where
R\Psi_{k_{1}/k_{0},\mathcal{P}}^{p}:D(k_{1,\mathrm{PRPS}})\to D(k_{0,\mathrm{et}}^{\mathrm{perar}})
is the derived pushforward of
\psi_{k_{1}/k_{0},\mathcal{P}}
.

Define
\mathcal{O}_{K,\mathrm{PRPS}}
as the category of flat
\mathcal{O}_{K}
-schemes with special fiber in
k_{1,\mathrm{PRPS}}
, with all
\mathcal{O}_{K}
-scheme morphisms and the étale topology. Define
w_{\mathcal{O}}:\mathcal{O}_{K,\mathrm{PRPS}}\to\mathcal{O}_{K,\mathrm{RPS}}
the premorphism defined by identity. We have a commutative diagram of premorphisms with horizontal maps defined by base change :

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K_{\mathrm{Et}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}_{K,\mathrm{PRPS}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces k_{1,\mathrm{PRPS}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces K_{\mathrm{Et}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{O}_{K,\mathrm{RPS}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces k_{1,\mathrm{RPS}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{\mathcal{P}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces w_{\mathcal{O}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{\mathcal{P}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces w}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}

which induces a natural map
Lw_{\mathcal{O}}^{*}Rj_{*}\to Rj_{\mathcal{P},*}
. Writing
R\Psi_{K/k_{1},\mathcal{P}}^{m}=Li_{\mathcal{P}}^{*}Rj_{\mathcal{P},*}
, we get a natural map :

w^{*}R\Psi_{K/k_{1}}^{m}=Li_{\mathcal{P}}^{*}Lw_{\mathcal{O}}^{*}Rj_{*}\to R\Psi_{K/k_{1},\mathcal{P}}^{m}.

By
[
21
]
(Cor. 3.1) the functor
A\mapsto A\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}k_{1}
from the category of flat
\mathcal{O}_{K}
-algebras with relatively perfect special fibers is left adjoint to
R\mapsto h^{\mathcal{O}_{K}/k_{1}}(R)
. In particular the restriction of
i_{\mathcal{P}}^{-1}:k_{1,\mathrm{PRPS}}\to\mathcal{O}_{K,\mathrm{PRPS}}
to affines has a right adjoint described by the Kato lift, so
i_{\mathcal{P}}^{*}
coincides on affine objects with the pushforward of the premorphism
T/k_{1}\mapsto h^{\mathcal{O}_{K}/k_{1}}(T)
. Hence
i_{\mathcal{P}}^{*}
is left exact and
R\Psi_{K/k_{1},\mathcal{P}}^{m}=i_{\mathcal{P}}^{*}Rj_{\mathcal{P},*}
.

This description of
i_{\mathcal{P}}^{*}
also shows that
R^{q}\Psi_{K/k_{1},\mathcal{P}}^{m}=H^{q}R\Psi_{K/k_{1},\mathcal{P}}
is the
q
-th left derived functor of
R^{0}\Psi_{K/k_{1},\mathcal{P}}^{m}
, and by Lemma
53
,
R^{q}\Psi_{K/k_{1},\mathcal{P}}^{m}(F)
coincides on affine objects with the sheafification of the presheaf
T/k_{1}\mapsto H^{q}(h^{K/k_{1}(T)},F)
of
k_{1,\mathrm{PRPS}}
, for
F\in\Sh(K_{\mathrm{Et}})
and
q\in\mathbb{Z}
.

We show
w^{*}R\Psi_{K/k_{1}}^{m}(G)\to R\Psi_{K/k_{1},\mathcal{P}}^{m}(G)
is an isomorphism for
G\in D_{\mathrm{fin}}(K_{\mathrm{Et}})
. It suffices to consider
G\in\Sh_{\mathrm{fin}}(K_{\mathrm{Et}})
. Let
T=\Spec R
be an affine object of
k_{1,\mathrm{PRPS}}
written as
R=\varinjlim_{\lambda}R_{\lambda}
with
R_{\lambda}\in k_{1,\mathrm{RPS}}
. Let
x=\varprojlim_{\lambda}x_{\lambda}
be a point of
T
, and
\overline{x}=\varprojlim_{\lambda}\overline{x}_{\lambda}
a separable closure. Let
q\in\mathbb{Z}
. Then :

\displaystyle(w^{*}R^{q}\Psi_{K/k_{1}}^{m}(G))_{\overline{x}}

\displaystyle=\varinjlim_{\lambda}R^{q}\Psi_{K/k_{1}}^{m}(G)_{\overline{x}_{\lambda}}

\displaystyle\text{by description of }w^{*},

\displaystyle=\varinjlim_{\lambda}\varinjlim_{S_{\lambda}/R_{\lambda}}H^{q}(h^{K/k_{1}}(S_{\lambda}),G)

by Lemma
64
.2,

\displaystyle=H^{q}(\varinjlim_{\lambda}\varinjlim_{S_{\lambda}/R_{\lambda}}h^{K/k_{1}}(S_{\lambda}),G)

by
[
25
]
Lem. III.1.16,

\displaystyle=H^{q}(h^{K/k_{1}}(\varinjlim_{\lambda}\varinjlim_{S_{\lambda}/R_{\lambda}}S_{\lambda}),G)

by
[
3
]
Cor. 1.18.2 and Lemma
96
,

\displaystyle=H^{q}(h^{K/k_{1}}(R_{x}^{sh}),G)

\displaystyle=\varinjlim_{S/R}H^{q}(h^{K/k_{1}}(S),G)

by
[
3
]
Cor. 1.18.2, Lemma
96
,
[
25
]
Lem. III.1.16

\displaystyle=(R^{q}\Psi_{K/k_{1},\mathcal{P}}^{m}(G))_{\overline{x}}

\displaystyle\text{by description of }R\Psi_{K/k_{1},\mathcal{P}}^{m}

where
S_{\lambda}/R_{\lambda}
(resp.
S/R
) runs over all étale neighborhoods of
x_{\lambda}
(resp.
x
). We repeatedly use
[
34
]
(Lem 04GW) and the corollary that
R_{x}^{sh}=\varinjlim_{\lambda}R_{\lambda,x_{\lambda}}^{sh}
. Here it is not necessary that
G
be upper bounded or with finite cohomology, but it is important that
G
be the pullback of a lower bounded sheaf on
K_{\mathrm{et}}
with torsion cohomology in order to use
[
3
]
Cor. 1.18.2.

Combining the above with the previous
R\Psi_{k_{1}/k_{0}}^{p}Lv^{*}=R\Psi_{k_{1}/k_{0},\mathcal{P}}^{p}w^{*}
, we get :

R\Psi_{K/k_{0}}(G)=R\Psi_{k_{1}/k_{0}}^{p}Lv^{*}R\Psi_{K/k_{1}}^{m}(G)=u^{*}R\Psi_{k_{1}/k_{0},\mathcal{P}}^{p}R\Psi_{K/k_{1},\mathcal{P}}^{m}(G).

But from their descriptions on affine objects as pushforwards of
T/k_{0}\mapsto h^{k_{1}/k_{0}}(T_{0})
and
T/k_{1}\mapsto h^{K/k_{1}}(T)
, it follows that the composite of
R\Psi_{k_{1}/k_{0},\mathcal{P}}^{p}
and
R\Psi_{K/k_{1},\mathcal{P}}^{m}
coincides with the pushforward of the premorphism
T/k_{0}\mapsto h^{K/k_{1}}(h^{k_{1}/k_{0}}(T))=h^{K/k_{0}}(T)
. This is the functor
R\tilde{\Psi}_{K/k_{0}}
, finishing the proof.

2.
By Lemma
82
,
Lv^{*}
and
Lv_{\mathcal{P}}^{*}
are monoidal. As seen in
(1.)
, we have isomorphisms for
G\in D_{\mathrm{fin}}(K_{\mathrm{et}})
:

\displaystyle R\Psi_{K/k_{0}}(G)

\displaystyle=R\Psi_{k_{1}/k_{0}}^{p}Lv^{*}R\Psi_{K/k_{1}}^{m}u^{*}(G),

\displaystyle R\tilde{\Psi}_{K/k_{0}}

\displaystyle=R\Psi_{k_{1}/k_{0},\mathcal{P}}^{p}R\Psi_{K/k_{1},\mathcal{P}}^{m},

\displaystyle w^{*}R\Psi_{K/k_{1}}^{m}(G)

\displaystyle=R\Psi_{K/k_{1},\mathcal{P}}^{m}(G),

\displaystyle R\Psi_{k_{1}/k_{0}}^{p}Lv^{*}

\displaystyle=R\Psi_{k_{1}/k_{0},\mathcal{P}}^{p}w^{*}.

In all cases the pairings are defined by using the monoidality of the (derived) pullbacks
u^{*}
,
Lv^{*}
,
i^{*}
,
i_{\mathcal{P}}^{*}
, and cup-products for pushforwards
R\Psi_{k_{1}/k_{0}}^{p}
,
Rj_{*}
,
R\Psi_{k_{1}/k_{0},\mathcal{P}}
,
Rj_{\mathcal{P},*}
and
R\tilde{\Psi}_{K/k_{0}}
. In particular the natural multiplicative structures of the left and right sides of the first equality coincide by definition, and we need to show the other three isomorphisms are compatible with the natural mutiplicative structures.

The formation of cup-products for pushforwards is compatible with the composition of premorphisms of sites, so multiplicative structures for
R\Psi_{k_{1}/k_{0},\mathcal{P}}^{p}R\Psi_{K/k_{1},\mathcal{P}}^{m}
and for
R\tilde{\Psi}_{K/k_{0}}
coincide.

The equality
R\Psi_{k_{1}/k_{0}}^{p}Lv^{*}=R\Psi_{k_{1}/k_{0},\mathcal{P}}^{p}w^{*}
comes from identifications
R\Psi_{k_{1}/k_{0}}^{p}=R\Psi_{k_{1}/k_{0},\mathcal{P}}^{p}v_{\mathcal{P},*}
and
v_{\mathcal{P},*}Lv^{*}=v_{\mathcal{P}}Lv_{\mathcal{P}}^{*}w^{*}=w^{*}
. The first is compatible with cup-products as before, by composition of pushforwards of premorphism of sites. The second comes from the unit
\mathrm{id}\to v_{\mathcal{P},*}Lv_{\mathcal{P}}^{*}
, which is multiplicative in the sense that we have a commutative diagram for
F,F^{\prime}\in\Sh(k_{1,\mathrm{PRPS}})
:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\otimes^{L}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F\otimes^{L}F^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces v_{\mathcal{P},*}Lv_{\mathcal{P}}^{*}F}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\otimes^{L}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces v_{\mathcal{P},*}Lv_{\mathcal{P}}^{*}F^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces v_{\mathcal{P},*}(Lv_{\mathcal{P}}^{*}F\otimes^{L}Lv_{\mathcal{P}}^{*}F^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces v_{\mathcal{P},*}Lv_{\mathcal{P}}^{*}(F\otimes^{L}F^{\prime})}

where the lower pairing is given by the cup-product of
v_{\mathcal{P},*}
and monoidality of
Lv_{\mathcal{P}}^{*}
; this can be seen using Lemma
82
.2. Hence the multiplicative structures for
R\Psi_{k_{1}/k_{0}}^{p}Lv^{*}
and
R\Psi_{k_{1}/k_{0},\mathcal{P}}^{p}w^{*}
coincide.

Let
G\in D_{\mathrm{fin}}(K_{\mathrm{Et}})
. For
q\in\mathbb{Z}
, when restricted to affines the sheaf
w^{*}R^{q}\Psi_{K/k_{1}}^{m}(G)
coincides with the sheafification of
T=\varprojlim_{i}T_{i}\mapsto\varinjlim_{i}H^{q}(h^{K/k_{1}}(T_{i}),G)
. On the other hand,
R^{q}\Psi_{K/k_{1},\mathcal{P}}^{m}(G)
when restricted to affines coincides with the sheafification of
T\mapsto H^{q}(h^{K/k_{1}}(T),G)
. By continuity of étale cohomology and
[
3
]
(Cor. 1.18.2), we have that the restriction map :

\varinjlim_{i}H^{q}(h^{K/k_{1}}(T_{i}),G)\to H^{q}(h^{K/k_{1}}(T),G)

is an isomorphism, which describes the equality
w^{*}R\Psi_{K/k_{1}}^{m}(G)=R\Psi_{K/k_{1},\mathcal{P}}^{m}(G)
locally. On the other hand, the cup-products for the restrictions of
R\Psi_{K/k_{1}}^{m}
and
R\Psi_{K/k_{1},\mathcal{P}}^{m}
to affines are both obtained by sheafifying the cup-products for these étale cohomology groups. In other words, for
F,F^{\prime}\in\Sh(K_{\mathrm{Et}})
and
q,r\in\mathbb{Z}
the following diagram :

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a(H^{q}(h^{K/k_{1}}(-),F)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\otimes}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{r}(h^{K/k_{1}}(-),F^{\prime}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a(H^{q+r}(h^{K/k_{1}}(-),F\otimes F^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R^{q}\Psi_{K/k_{1}}^{m}(F)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\otimes}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R^{r}\Psi_{K/k_{1}}(F^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R^{q}\Psi_{K/k_{1}}^{m}(F\otimes F^{\prime})}

commutes when viewed as a diagram of sheaves over affine objects of
k_{1,\mathrm{RPS}}
; the analogous diagram for
R^{q}\Psi_{K/k_{1},\mathcal{P}}^{m}
also commutes over affine objects of
k_{1,\mathrm{PRPS}}
. Hence the compatibility of the isomorphism
w^{*}R\Psi_{K/k_{1}}^{m}(G)\xrightarrow{\sim}R\Psi_{K/k_{1},\mathcal{P}}^{m}(G)
with multiplicative structures comes from the commutativity of the diagram of cup-products :

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varinjlim_{i}H^{q}(h^{K/k_{1}}(T_{i}),F)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\otimes}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varinjlim_{i}H^{r}(h^{K/k_{1}}(T_{i}),F^{\prime}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\varinjlim_{i}H^{q+r}(h^{K/k_{1}}(T_{i}),F\otimes^{L}F^{\prime})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{q}(h^{K/k_{1}}(T),F)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\otimes}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{r}(h^{K/k_{1}}(T),F^{\prime}))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{q+r}(h^{K/k_{1}}(T),F\otimes F^{\prime})}

for
T=\varprojlim_{i}T_{i}
an affine object of
k_{1,\mathrm{PRPS}}
, which follows from the compatibility of the tensor product with colimits and of the restrictions
H^{q}(h^{K/k_{1}}(T_{i}),-)\to H^{q}(h^{K/k_{1}}(T),-)
with cup-products. This concludes.

3.
The exactness of
\beta_{k_{0},*}
, which is simply the restriction to
k_{0,\mathrm{et}}
, is clear. By Lemma
53
,
R^{q}\Psi^{\mathrm{et}}_{U/k_{0}}(G)
is the sheafification of
T/k_{0}\mapsto H^{q}(U_{h^{K/k_{0}}(T)},G)
and
R^{q}\Psi^{\mathrm{et}}_{U/k_{0},c}(G)
is that of :

T/k_{0}\mapsto H^{q}(X_{h^{K/k_{0}}(T)},(j_{!}G)|_{X_{h^{K/k_{0}}(T)}})=H^{q}_{c}(U_{h^{K/k_{0}}(T)},G|_{U_{h^{K/k_{0}}(T)}})

where
(j_{!}G)|_{X_{h^{K/k_{0}}(T)}}=j_{h^{K/k_{0}}(T),!}(G|_{U_{h^{K/k_{0}}(T)}})
is by base change. The identification of stalks follows and it remains to compare
R\Psi_{U/k_{0}}
with
R\Psi_{U/k_{0}}^{\mathrm{et}}
(and
R\Psi_{U/k_{0},c}
with
R\Psi_{U/k_{0},c}^{\mathrm{et}}
). By Lemma
56
and Theorem
76
.4, we have identifications in
D(k_{2,\mathrm{et}})
:

\displaystyle R\Psi_{k_{3/k_{2}}}^{0}\circ\cdots\circ R\Psi_{k_{d}/k_{d-1}}^{0}\circ R\Psi_{U/K}^{v}(G)_{k_{2}^{\mathrm{sep}}}

\displaystyle=R\Gamma(k_{3}^{\mathrm{ur}},-)\circ\cdots\circ R\Gamma(k_{d}^{\mathrm{ur}},-)\circ R\Gamma(U_{K^{\mathrm{sep}}},-)(G),

\displaystyle R\Psi_{k_{3/k_{2}}}^{0}\circ\cdots\circ R\Psi_{k_{d}/k_{d-1}}^{0}\circ R\Psi_{U/K,c}^{v}(G)_{k_{2}^{\mathrm{sep}}}

\displaystyle=R\Gamma(k_{3}^{\mathrm{ur}},-)\circ\cdots\circ R\Gamma(k_{d}^{\mathrm{ur}},-)\circ R\Gamma_{c}(U_{K^{\mathrm{sep}}},-)(G),

while by
[
25
]
(Prop. III.2.20) we also have :

\displaystyle R\Gamma(U_{K^{\mathrm{tur}}},G|_{U^{\mathrm{tur}}})

\displaystyle=R\Gamma(k_{2}^{\mathrm{tur}},-)\circ R\Gamma(k_{3}^{\mathrm{ur}},-)\circ\cdots\circ R\Gamma(k_{d}^{\mathrm{ur}},-)\circ R\Gamma(U_{K^{\mathrm{sep}}},-)(G),

\displaystyle R\Gamma_{c}(U_{K^{\mathrm{tur}}},G|_{U^{\mathrm{tur}}})

\displaystyle=R\Gamma(k_{2}^{\mathrm{tur}},-)\circ R\Gamma(k_{3}^{\mathrm{ur}},-)\circ\cdots\circ R\Gamma(k_{d}^{\mathrm{ur}},-)\circ R\Gamma_{c}(U_{K^{\mathrm{sep}}},-)(G).

which reduces us to proving
\beta_{k_{0},*}\circ R\Psi_{k_{2}/k_{0}}(G)=R\Psi^{\mathrm{et}}_{k_{2}/k_{0}}(G)
. This follows directly from
(1.)
.

4.
This is
(2.)
plus the fact that, in
(3.)
, all new identifications are compatible with cup-products.
∎

Proposition 84
.

Consider Situation
78
. Assume
k_{0}
is finite. Recall
R\Psi_{U/*}
and
R\Psi_{U/*,c}
from Theorem
81
.

1.

For
G\in D_{\mathrm{fin}}(U_{\mathrm{et}})
and
q\in\mathbb{Z}
we have canonical isomorphisms :

R^{q}\Psi_{U/*}(G)(*)=H^{q}(U_{\mathrm{et}},G),\qquad R^{q}\Psi_{U/*,c}(G)(*)=H^{q}_{c}(U_{\mathrm{et}},G)=H^{q}(X_{\mathrm{et}},j_{!}G).

2.

For
G\in D_{\mathrm{fin}}(U_{\mathrm{et}})
and
G^{\vee}=R\HOM_{U_{\mathrm{et}}}(G,\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z}(d+e))
, there is a commutative diagram of pairings :

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R^{q}\Psi_{U/*}(G)(*)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\otimes}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R^{d+2e+1-q}\Psi_{U/*,c}(G^{\vee})(*)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R^{d+2e+1}\Psi_{U/*,c}(\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z}(d+e))(*)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{q}(U_{\mathrm{et}},G)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\otimes}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{q}_{c}(U_{\mathrm{et}},G^{\vee})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{d+2e+1}_{c}(U_{\mathrm{et}},\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z}(d+e))}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

Proof of Proposition
84
.

Consider the diagram :

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D(U_{\mathrm{et}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D(k_{0,\mathrm{et}}^{\mathrm{perar}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D(k_{0,\mathrm{proet}}^{\mathrm{indrat}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D(*_{\mathrm{proet}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D(U_{\mathrm{et}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D(k_{0,\mathrm{et}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D(k_{0,\mathrm{et}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D(\mathrm{Ab})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Psi_{U/k_{0}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha_{k_{0}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)_{\overline{k}_{0}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Psi_{k_{0}/*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)_{\overline{k}_{0}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma(*,-)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma(U_{K^{\mathrm{tur}}},-)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R\Gamma(k_{0},-)}

where
\alpha_{k_{0}}:D(k_{0,\mathrm{et}}^{\mathrm{perar}})\to D(k_{0,\mathrm{proet}}^{\mathrm{indrat}})
is the change of site functor. The leftmost square commutes by Proposition
83
.3. The second square commutes when restricted to
D^{b}_{\mathcal{W}_{k_{0}}}(k_{0,\mathrm{et}}^{\mathrm{perar}})
or
D_{\mathrm{fin}}(k_{0,\mathrm{et}}^{\mathrm{perar}})
, by Proposition
41
.4 or Lemma
56
.4. From the definition of
R\Psi_{k_{0}/*}
we clearly have
R\Gamma(*,R\Psi_{k_{0}/*}(-))=R\Gamma(k_{0,\mathrm{proet}},-)
, so the third square commutes by the same results when restricted to
\langle\mathcal{W}_{k_{0}}\rangle_{k_{0,\mathrm{proet}}^{\mathrm{indrat}}}
or
D_{\mathrm{fin}}(k_{0,\mathrm{proet}}^{\mathrm{indrat}})
.

The composite of the top arrows is exactly
R\Psi_{U/*}
. The composite of the bottom arrows is precisely
R\Gamma(U,-)
, by
[
25
]
(Prop. III.2.20). By Theorem
79
.1, Proposition
41
.3 and Lemma
56
, we thus have :

R\Gamma(*,R\Psi_{U/*}(G))=R\Gamma(U,G),\qquad G\in D_{\mathrm{fin}}(U_{\mathrm{et}}).

By Proposition
51
.1,
\Gamma(*,-):\Sh(*_{\mathrm{proet}})\to\mathrm{Ab}
is exact hence
R^{q}\Psi_{U/*}(G)(*)=H^{q}(U,G)
for
G\in D_{\mathrm{fin}}(U_{\mathrm{et}})
. The identification
R^{q}\Psi_{U/*,c}(G)(*)=H^{q}_{c}(U,G)
is identical, with the addition of a base change argument to show
R\Gamma(k_{0},-)\circ R\Gamma_{c}(U_{K^{\mathrm{tur}}},-)=R\Gamma_{c}(U,-)
.

This identification respects cup-products because the three following pairs identifications do :

\displaystyle R\Psi_{U/k_{0}}(G)_{\overline{k}_{0}}=R\Gamma(U_{K^{\mathrm{tur}}},G),\qquad

\displaystyle R\Psi_{U/k_{0},c}(G^{\vee})_{\overline{k}_{0}}=R\Gamma_{c}(U_{K^{\mathrm{tur}}},G^{\vee}),

\displaystyle\alpha_{k_{0}}(H)_{\overline{k}_{0}}=H_{\overline{k}_{0}},\qquad

\displaystyle\alpha_{k_{0}}(H^{\vee})_{\overline{k}_{0}}=(H^{\vee})_{\overline{k}_{0}},

\displaystyle R\Gamma(*,R\Psi_{k_{0}/*}(W))=R\Gamma(k_{0},W_{\overline{k}_{0}}),\qquad

\displaystyle R\Gamma(*,R\Psi_{k_{0}/*}(W^{\vee}))=R\Gamma(k_{0},(W^{\vee})_{\overline{k}_{0}}),

for
G\in D_{\mathrm{fin}}(U_{\mathrm{et}})
,
H\in D_{\mathrm{fin}}(k_{0,\mathrm{et}}^{\mathrm{perar}})
or
D^{b}_{\mathcal{W}_{k_{0}}}(k_{0,\mathrm{et}}^{\mathrm{perar}})
, and
W\in D_{\mathrm{fin}}(k_{0,\mathrm{proet}}^{\mathrm{indrat}})
or
D^{b}_{\mathcal{W}_{k_{0}}}(k_{0,\mathrm{proet}}^{\mathrm{indrat}})
, where
(-)^{\vee}=R\HOM(-,\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z}(r))
with
r=d+e,0,0
respectively for
G,H,W
. The compatibility for the first pair is by Proposition
83
.4, the second by Theorem
43
.1, the third by composition of pushforwards.
∎

The following corollary now gives a reformulation of Theorem
81
with classical objects.

Corollary 85
.

Let
K
be a
d
-local field of mixed characteristic at level
2
, with finite
0
-local residue field. Let
X
be a smooth, proper, geometrically integral
K
-scheme of dimension
e
and
U\subseteq X
a nonempty open. Let
G\in D_{\mathrm{fin}}(U_{\mathrm{et}})
and
G^{\vee}=R\HOM_{U_{\mathrm{et}}}(G,\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z}(d+e))
. Then there exist canonical, functorial, locally compact Hausdorff group topologies on
H^{q}(U,G)
and
H^{q}_{c}(U,G^{\vee})
, for
q\in\mathbb{Z}
, such that the cup-product :

H^{q}(U,G)\otimes H^{d+2e+1-q}_{c}(U,G^{\vee})\to H^{d+2e+1}(U,\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z}(d+e))\to\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z}

is a perfect Pontryagin duality. In particular, it is nondegenerate.

Remark 86
.

In Situation
77
we consider a
d
-local field
K=k_{d},\dots,k_{0}
with mixed characteristic
(0,p)
at level
2
. With the same methods we can in fact consider the cases where
K
has mixed characteristic at level
1
or totally equal characteristic
0
.

1.

If
K
has mixed characteristic at level
1
, define :

\displaystyle R\Psi_{U/k_{0}}

\displaystyle=w_{*}\circ Lv^{*}\circ R\Psi_{k_{1}/k_{0}}^{m}\circ u^{*}\circ R\Psi_{k_{2}/k_{1}}^{0}\circ\cdots\circ R\Psi_{k_{d}/k_{d-1}}^{0}\circ R\Psi_{U/K}^{v},

\displaystyle R\Psi_{U/k_{0},c}

\displaystyle=w_{*}\circ Lv^{*}\circ R\Psi_{k_{1}/k_{0}}^{m}\circ u^{*}\circ R\Psi_{k_{2}/k_{1}}^{0}\circ\cdots\circ R\Psi_{k_{d}/k_{d-1}}^{0}\circ R\Psi_{U/K,c}^{v}

as functors
D(U_{\mathrm{et}})\to D(k_{0,\mathrm{et}}^{\mathrm{perar}})
, where
w:k_{0,\mathrm{RP}}\to k_{0,\mathrm{et}}^{\mathrm{perar}}
,
v:k_{0,\mathrm{RP}}\to k_{0,\mathrm{RPS}}
and
u:k_{1,\mathrm{Et}}\to k_{1,\mathrm{et}}
are induced by identity. Then Theorem
79
, Theorem
81
, Proposition
83
.3-4 and Corollary
85
still hold verbatim. In fact in Theorem
79
,
D_{p}(U_{\mathrm{et}})
maps to the smaller category
D^{b}(\Alg_{u}^{\mathrm{RP}}(k_{0}))\subseteq D^{b}_{\mathcal{W}_{k_{0}}}(k_{0,\mathrm{et}}^{\mathrm{perar}})
, and in Theorem
81
,
D_{p}(U_{\mathrm{et}})
maps to the smaller category
D_{p}(*_{\mathrm{proet}})\subseteq D^{b}_{\mathcal{W}_{p}}(*_{\mathrm{proet}})
. In particular, the pairing in Corollary
85
is a perfect pairing of finite groups, recovering results of
[
16
]
. The only differences with the treated cases are that we need
w_{*}
to satisfy a duality (this is Proposition
41
.2 and Theorem
43
.3.a), and that Proposition
83
.1-2 must be adapted for
d=1
.

2.

If
K
has totally equal characteristic
0
, define :

\displaystyle R\Psi_{U/k_{0}}

\displaystyle=R\Psi_{k_{1}/k_{0}}^{0}\circ\cdots\circ R\Psi_{k_{d}/k_{d-1}}^{0}\circ R\Psi_{U/K}^{v},

\displaystyle R\Psi_{U/k_{0},c}

\displaystyle=R\Psi_{k_{1}/k_{0}}^{0}\circ\cdots\circ R\Psi_{k_{d}/k_{d-1}}^{0}\circ R\Psi_{U/K}^{v}

as functors
D(U_{\mathrm{et}})\to D(k_{0,\mathrm{et}})
. Then Theorem
79
adapts in the following form.

—

If
G\in D_{\mathrm{fin}}(U_{\mathrm{et}})
then
R\Psi_{U/k_{0}}(G)
and
R\Psi_{U/k_{0},c}(G)
belong to
D_{\mathrm{fin}}(k_{0,\mathrm{et}})
. If
G\in\Sh_{\mathrm{fin}}(U_{\mathrm{et}})
, then
R\Psi_{U/k_{0}}(G)
and
R\Psi_{U/k_{0},c}(G)
are concentrated in degrees
0,\dots,d+2e
.

—

If
G\in D_{\mathrm{fin}}(U_{\mathrm{et}})
and
G^{\vee}=R\HOM_{U_{\mathrm{et}}}(G,\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z}(d+2))
then we have a perfect Cartier duality :

R\Psi_{U/k_{0}}(G)\otimes^{L}R\Psi_{U/k_{0},c}(G^{\vee})\to R\Psi_{U/k_{0},c}(\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z}(d+e))\to\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z}[-d-2e].

—

For
G\in D_{\mathrm{fin}}(U_{\mathrm{et}})
, we have
R\Psi_{U/k_{0}}(G)=R\Gamma(U_{K^{\mathrm{tur}}},G)
and
R\Psi_{U/k_{0},c}(G)=R_{c}\Gamma(U_{K^{\mathrm{tur}}},G)
in
\Sh(k_{0,\mathrm{et}})
.

If in addition
k_{0}
is quasi-finite, meaning we have an isomorphism
\Gal(k^{\mathrm{sep}}_{0}/k_{0})\cong\hat{\mathbb{Z}}
(for instance,
k_{0}=\mathbb{C}(\!(t)\!)
) then the functor
R\Gamma(k_{0},-):D(k_{0,\mathrm{et}})\to D(\mathrm{Ab})
sends
D_{\mathrm{fin}}(k_{0,\mathrm{et}})
to
D^{b}(\mathrm{Ab}_{\mathrm{fin}})
, and Cartier dualities in
D_{\mathrm{fin}}(k_{0,\mathrm{et}})
to perfect pairings of finite groups, via a trace map
H^{1}(k_{0},\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})=\Hom_{\text{cont}}(\Gal(k_{0}^{\mathrm{sep}}/k_{0}),\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z})\xrightarrow{\sim}\mathbb{Q}/\mathbb{Z}
. Thus Corollary
85
also holds in this case, where the pairing of étale cohomology groups is in fact a perfect pairing of (discrete) finite groups as in
[
16
]
.

By contrast, the methods in this paper are insufficient to consider cases where
\characteristic k_{3}>0
, because we lack a positive equal characteristic step with imperfect residue field. Similarly, though we have a duality for
1
-local fields of equal characteristic
p>0
, we cannot consider varieties
U
over them because we lack a
p
-torsion version of Poincaré duality.

Appendix : Kato lifting

In this appendix we review Kato’s canonical lifting.

Lemma 87
.

Let
A
be a ring and
I
a nilpotent ideal. Write
k=A/I
the quotient ring. Consider
A
-algebras
B
and
B^{\prime}
. Assume
B
is formally étale, and assume
B^{\prime}
is either formally étale or flat, over
A
. Then any isomorphism
B/IB\xrightarrow{\sim}B^{\prime}/IB^{\prime}
over
k
lifts uniquely to an isomorphism
B\xrightarrow{\sim}B^{\prime}
over
A
.

Proof of Lemma
87
.

By Yoneda’s lemma, a formally étale
A
-algebra
B
is determined by the
k
-algebra
B/IB
since :

\Hom_{A}(B,C)=\Hom_{A}(B,C/IC)=\Hom_{k}(B/IB,C/IC)

for any
A
-algebra
C
. This solves the case
B^{\prime}
is formally étale.

Now assume
B
is formally étale and
B^{\prime}
is flat. The formal étaleness of
B
ensures there exists a unique lift
B\to B^{\prime}
of the isomorphism
B/IB\xrightarrow{\sim}B^{\prime}/IB^{\prime}
, as before. Once such a lift is given and
B^{\prime}
is flat, the morphism
B\to B^{\prime}
is an isomorphism which can be seen as follows. Let
K
and
C
be its kernel and cokernel as a morphism of
A
-modules. Then
C/IC
is the cokernel of
B/IB\to B^{\prime}/IB^{\prime}
, by right-exactness of
-\otimes_{A}A/I
, so
C=IC
. It follows that
C=I^{n}C
for all
n\geq 0
, and by nilpotence of
I
,
C=0
. Thus
B\to B^{\prime}
is surjective. By flatness of
B^{\prime}
, we now have
K/IK=\ker(B/IB\to B^{\prime}/IB^{\prime})=0
and as for
C
,
K=IK=I^{2}K=\dots=0
. Hence
B\to B^{\prime}
is bijective.
3
3

3

This argument, omitted from the original construction
[
18
]
, can be found in Jarod Alper’s
Notes on Deformation Theory
(
https://sites.math.washington.edu/~{}jarod/courses/math581J-fall21/def-theory-notes.pdf
at the time of writing.)

∎

Proposition 88
.

Let
A
be a ring,
I
a nilpotent ideal,
k=A/I
and
R
a
k
-algebra. Assume that either :

1.

k
has prime characteristic
p>0
and
R
is relatively perfect over
k
;

2.

the quotient
A\to k
admits a ring section
k\to A
and
R
is formally étale over
k
.

Then there exists a unique formally étale
A
-algebra
B
equipped with a
k
-isomorphism
B\otimes_{A}k\cong R
. If
R
is
k
-flat, then
B
is the unique
A
-flat algebra equipped with a
k
-isomorphism
B\otimes_{A}k\cong R
.

Proof of Proposition
88
.

In any case, uniqueness comes from Lemma
87
. For the existence in case
(1.)
, see
[
18
]
Lem. 1 : the algebra
B
can be constructed by endowing
A
with a certain
W_{N}(k)
-algebra structure and taking
B=A\otimes_{W_{N}(k)}W_{N}(R)
, for
N\geq 1
greater than the nilpotence index of
I
, where
W_{N}
denotes
p
-typical Witt vectors. For the existence in case
(2.)
, simply set
B=R\otimes_{k}A
given any section
k\to A
: it is formally étale (resp. flat) over
A
by base change, if
R
is.
∎

Proposition 89
.

Let
A
be a Noetherian, complete ring with respect to an ideal
I
(meaning
A=\varprojlim_{n}A/I^{n}
),
k=A/I
and
R
a
k
-algebra. Consider one of the following cases :

1.

k
has prime characteristic
p>0
and
R
is flat relatively perfect over
k
;

2.

the quotient
A\to k
admits a ring section
k\to A
and
R
is flat formally étale over
k
.

Then there exists a unique flat,
I
-adically complete
A
-algebra
B
equipped with a
k
-isomorphism
B\otimes_{A}k\cong R
.

Proof of Proposition
89
.

For each
n\geq 1
, there exists a unique flat
A/I^{n}
-algebra
B_{n}
such that
B_{n}\otimes_{A}k=R
, by Proposition
88
. Then
B=\varprojlim_{n}B_{n}
is
A
-flat by
[
34
]
(Lem. 0912). For
n\geq 1
, by the same reference,
B/I^{n}B=\varprojlim_{m\geq n}B_{m}/I^{n}B_{m}
and
B_{m}/I^{n}B_{m}=B_{n}
by uniqueness in Proposition
88
, so
B
is
I
-complete. The uniqueness of
B
follows Proposition
89
and the fact that
B
is determined by the
B/I^{n}B
’s.
∎

Remark 90
.

The assumption of Proposition
89
.2 on
A
is satisfied, for instance, if
A
is a complete local ring with residue field
k
of characteristic
0
, by the Cohen structure theorem (
[
34
]
proof of Th. 032A),
A
is a quotient of a power series ring over
k
. If
k
has characteristic
p>0
, Proposition
89
.1 is more general.

Definition 91
.

With
A
,
I
,
k
,
R
and
B
as in Proposition
88
or Proposition
89
, the algebra
B
is called the
Kato lift of
R
over
A
and written
h^{A/k}(R)
. In the case of Proposition
89
if
A
is an integral domain with fraction field
K
, we also write
h^{K/k}(R)=h^{A/k}(R)\otimes_{A}K
and
h^{A/k}_{n}(R)=h^{(A/I^{n})/k}(R)
.

Remark 92
.

The Kato lift can be partially generalized to non-affine
k
-schemes.

1.

Bertapelle and Suzuki generalized the finite level Kato lift of Proposition
88
to non-affine schemes : if
X
is a scheme,
I
a sheaf of locally nilpotent ideals containing
p
, and
Z\to X
the corresponding closed immersion, then any
T\in\mathrm{RPSch}/Z
lifts uniquely to a formally étale
X
-scheme
h^{X/Z}(T)
such that
h^{X/Z}(T)\times_{X}Z=T
, and
h^{X/Z}
preserves fiber products and Zariski coverings (
[
2
]
Prop. 3.2).

2.

By contrast, the infinite level Kato lift of Proposition
89
does not extend reasonably to (even quasicompact quasiseparated) schemes. This is because the functor
h^{A/k}
from affine relatively perfect
k
-schemes to affine
A
-schemes does not commute with fiber products : for relatively perfect
k
-algebras
R,R^{\prime},S
we have
h^{A/k}(R\otimes_{S}R^{\prime})=[h^{A/k}(R)\otimes_{h^{A/k}(S)}h^{A/k}(R^{\prime})]^{\wedge}
and this differs from
h^{A/k}(R)\otimes_{h^{A/k}(S)}h^{A/k}(R^{\prime})
in general, for example if
S=k
and
R
and
R^{\prime}
are both infinite rank over
k
. Instead the infinite level Kato lift generalizes only as an ind-scheme. Consider an ind-scheme
X=\varinjlim_{n}X_{n}
whose transitions are infinitesimal thickenings, and such that the sheaf of ideals
I
defined by
X_{0}
contains
p
and is Zariski-locally nilpotent over each
X_{n}
. Then any relatively perfect
X_{0}
-scheme
T
lifts to an ind-scheme over
X
by the formula
h^{X/X_{0}}(T)=\varinjlim_{n}h^{X_{n}/X_{0}}(T)
. It is uniquely determined as an
X
-ind-scheme
Y
that is formally étale, complete in the sense that each
Y\times_{X}X_{n}
is a scheme and
Y=\varinjlim_{n}Y\times_{X}X_{n}
, and satisfies
Y\times_{X}X_{0}=T
.

Remark 93
.

In some cases, the Kato lift can be characterized in equivalent and more simple ways.

1.

By
[
19
]
(Prop. 5.2), if
S
is a locally Noetherian regular scheme, then any relatively perfect
S
-scheme is automatically
S
-flat. Thus in Remark
92
.1 if
Z
is locally Noetherian regular then
h^{X/Z}(T)
is always characterized as a flat
X
-scheme with
h^{X/Z}(T)\times_{X}Z=T
. This applies to Proposition
88
if
k
is regular Noetherian, such as a field.

2.

In Proposition
89
, if
A
is a complete discrete valuation ring with uniformizer
\pi
of equicharacteristic
p>0
then we have an isomorphism
A\cong k[\![\pi]\!]
and
h^{A/k}(R)
is given as
R[\![\pi]\!]=\varprojlim_{n}R[\pi]/(\pi^{n})
.

Lemma 94
.

Let
k
be a ring of prime characteristic
p>0
and
R
a
k
-algebra.

1.

If
k
is reduced and
R
is flat relatively perfect over
k
, then
R
is reduced.

2.

If
k
is reduced with
p
-basis
X
, and
R
is a reduced
k
-algebra, then
R
is relatively perfect over
k
if and only if
X
is a
p
-basis of
R
.

3.

If
k
is a field such that
[k:k^{p}]<\infty
and
R
is the relative perfection of a smooth
k
-algebra, then
R
is a finite product of integral domains, which are relatively perfectly smooth over
k
.

Proof of Lemma
94
.

For
B
an
\mathbb{F}_{p}
-algebra, write
B^{(p)}
the ring
B
with
B
-algebra structure given by the absolute Frobenius
F_{B}:B\to B
.

1.
The absolute Frobenius of
R
is the composite :

R=R\otimes_{k}k\xrightarrow{1\otimes F_{k}}R\otimes_{k}k^{(p)}\xrightarrow{F_{R/k}}R

where
F_{R/k}:x\otimes y\mapsto yx^{p}
is the relative Frobenius of
R
over
k
. Then
F_{k}
is injective because
k
is reduced, so
R\to R\otimes_{k}k^{(p)}
is injective by flatness,
F_{R/k}
is injective by relative perfection, so
F_{R}
is injective.

2.
The relative Frobenius is an
R
-module morphism when viewed as a map
R\otimes_{k}k^{(p)}\to R^{(p)}
. For a reduced
\mathbb{F}_{p}
-algebra
B
(such as
k
or
R
) a
p
-basis
Y
of
B
is exactly a subset of
B
such that the family
\tilde{Y}=\{x_{1}^{m_{1}}\cdots x_{r}^{m_{r}}\}_{x_{i}\in X,0<m_{i}<p}
defines a free basis of
B^{(p)}
as a
B
-module.

If
X
is a
p
-basis of
k
, then
k^{(p)}\cong k^{\oplus\tilde{X}}
and
R\otimes_{k}k^{\oplus\tilde{X}}\cong R^{\oplus\tilde{X}}
as an
R
-module. The relative Frobenius is an (
R
-module) isomorphism if and only if the free
R
-basis
\tilde{X}
of
R^{\oplus\tilde{X}}
is mapped to a free
R
-basis of
R^{(p)}
. One can see that a basis element
1\cdot x\in R^{\oplus\tilde{X}}\cong R\otimes k^{(p)}
is mapped to
1^{p}x=x\in R
under the relative Frobenius. Thus
\tilde{X}
is mapped to a free
R
-basis of
R^{(p)}
under the relative Frobenius if and only if
X
is mapped to a
p
-basis of
R
, which concludes.

3.
Write
R
as the relative perfection of a smooth
k
-algebra
R_{0}
. Then
\Spec R_{0}
is Noetherian hence has finitely many irreducible components. By
[
2
]
(Prop. 8.13),
\Spec R
is the (scheme theoretic) coproduct of finitely many irreducible components. In particular those are open in
\Spec R
, hence again relatively perfectly smooth over
k
. Thus
R=R_{1}\times\cdots\times R_{n}
with
R_{i}
relatively perfectly smooth over
k
and irreducible. By
(1.)
,
R_{i}
is also reduced, hence it is an integral domain by
[
34
]
(Lem. 01ON).
∎

Proposition 95
.

Consider
A,I,k,R
as in either case of Proposition
89
. Let
J\subseteq R
be an ideal and
I_{h^{A/k}(R),J}=\ker(h^{A/k}(R)\to R\to R/J)
.

1.

The pair
(h^{A/k}(R),Ih^{A/k}(R))
is Henselian. If
(R,J)
is Henselian then so is
(h^{A/k}(R),I_{h^{A/k}(R)})
.

2.

Assume
A
is discrete valuation with fraction field
K
and uniformizer
\pi
, and
I=\pi A
. Then :

h^{A/k}(R)=\{0\}\sqcup\bigsqcup_{n\geq 0}\pi^{n}\left[h^{A/k}(R)\setminus\pi h^{A/k}(R)\right],\qquad h^{K/k}(R)=\{0\}\sqcup\bigsqcup_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\pi^{n}\left[h^{A/k}(R)\setminus\pi h^{A/k}(R)\right].

If
R
is a finite product of integral domains then there is a split exact sequence :

0\to h^{A/k}(R)^{\times}\to h^{K/k}(R)^{\times}\to\Gamma(R,\mathbb{Z})\to 0

and if
R
is a field then
h^{A/k}(R)
is a discrete valuation ring with fraction field
h^{K/k}(R)
.

Proof of Proposition
95
.

1.
The first statement comes from
[
34
]
(Lem. 0ALJ) and the second from
[
34
]
(Lem. 0DYD,
(1)\Rightarrow(2)
).

2.
For
x\in h^{A/k}(R)
, either
x=0
or there exists some
n\geq 0
such that
x\notin\pi^{n+1}h^{A/k}(R)
, since
h^{A/k}(R)
is
I
-separated. Taking such an
n
minimal, we have :

h^{A/k}(R)=\{0\}\cup\bigcup_{n\geq 0}\left[\pi^{n}h^{A/k}(R)\setminus\pi^{n+1}h^{A/k}(R)\right]

Since
h^{A/k}(R)
is
A
-flat and
A
is integral, the map
x\mapsto\pi x
is injective on
h^{A/k}(R)
. Hence
\pi^{n}h^{A/k}(R)\setminus\pi^{n+1}h^{A/k}(R)=\pi^{n}\left[h^{A/k}(R)\setminus\pi h^{A/k}(R)\right]
. If
\pi^{n}a=\pi^{m}b
for some integers
n\geq m\geq 0
and
a,b\in h^{A/k}(R)\setminus\pi h^{A/k}(R)
, then we have
a=\pi^{m-n}b
so
m=n
(because
a\notin\pi h^{A/k}(R)
) and
a=b
. Hence a disjoint union :

h^{A/k}(R)=\{0\}\sqcup\bigsqcup_{n\geq 0}\pi^{n}\left[h^{A/k}(R)\setminus\pi h^{A/k}(R)\right].

Since the tensor product of
A
-modules preserves colimits and
K=\bigcup_{n\geq 0}\pi^{-n}A
, we have
h^{K/k}(R)=\bigcup_{n\geq 0}\pi^{-n}h^{A/k}(R)
and the previous decomposition extends as :

h^{K/k}(R)=\{0\}\sqcup\bigsqcup_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}\pi^{n}\left[h^{A/k}(R)\setminus\pi h^{A/k}(R)\right].

If
R
is a finite product of integral domains, we reduce to
R
integral by the observation that the factors of
R
are also flat relatively perfect (resp. flat formally étale)
k
-algebras, and the functor
h^{A/k}
commutes with finite products of such algebras. If
R
is integral consider
x\in h^{K/k}(R)^{\times}
. By the above there exist unique
n,m\in\mathbb{Z}
and elements
a,b\in h^{A/k}(R)\setminus\pi h^{A/k}(R)
such that
x=\pi^{n}a
and
x^{-1}=\pi^{m}b
. Then
1=xx^{-1}=\pi^{n+m}ab
and
ab\in h^{A/k}(R)\setminus\pi h^{A/k}(R)
because
h^{A/k}(R)/\pi h^{A/k}(R)=R
is integral. By uniqueness of the decomposition,
n=-m
and
ab=1
. This shows
x
decomposes uniquely as
x=\pi^{n}a
with
a\in h^{A/k}(R)^{\times}
. The claim follows.

If
R
is a field then
h^{A/k}(R)
is local with maximal ideal
\pi h^{A/k}(R)
by
(1.)
, hence
h^{K/k}(R)=h^{A/k}(R)[\pi^{-1}]
is its fraction field and the above shows any nonzero
x\in h^{A/k}(R)
(which becomes invertible in
h^{K/k}(R)
) is of the form
\pi^{n}a
with
a\in h^{A/k}(R)^{\times}
and some
n\geq 0
. This means
h^{A/k}(R)
is discrete valuation.
∎

We finish with a couple useful lemmas about the Kato lift functor.

Lemma 96
.

Consider
A,I,k
as in Proposition
89
and
\{R_{\lambda}\}
a filtered diagram of
k
-algebras all satisfying the assumptions of one case of Proposition
89
. Let
R=\varinjlim_{\lambda}R_{\lambda}
. Then
R
satisfies the same assumptions and
h^{A/k}(R)
is the
I
-completion of the
A
-algebra
\varinjlim_{\lambda}h^{A/k}(R_{\lambda})
.

Proof of Lemma
96
.

A filtered colimit of flat relatively perfect (resp. flat formally étale)
k
-algebras is again relatively perfect (resp. formally étale), and is flat by
[
34
]
(Lem. 05UU). Let
B_{\lambda}=h^{A/k}(R_{\lambda})
and
B=\varinjlim_{\lambda}B_{\lambda}
. Then
B
is
A
-flat by
[
34
]
(Lem. 05UU) and
B\otimes_{A}k=\varinjlim_{\lambda}B_{\lambda}\otimes_{A}k=R
. By
[
34
]
(Lem. 0912 and Lem. 031C), the
I
-completion
\hat{B}
is
A
-flat,
I
-complete and
\hat{B}\otimes_{A}k=B\otimes_{A}k=R
. We conclude by uniqueness of the Kato lift.
∎

Lemma 97
.

Let
K
be a complete discrete valuation field with residue field
k
. Consider
L/K
a finite extension with residue field
l
and
R/k
an algebra. Assume
R
is relatively perfect over
k
if
\characteristic k=p>0
, or étale in general. Then there are canonical isomorphisms of
\mathcal{O}_{L}
- and
L
-algebras :

h^{\mathcal{O}_{L}/l}(R\otimes_{k}l)=h^{\mathcal{O}_{K}/k}(R)\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}\mathcal{O}_{L},\qquad h^{L/l}(R\otimes_{k}l)=h^{K/k}(R)\otimes_{K}L.

Proof of Lemma
97
.

The second equality will follow from the first by associativity of the tensor product :
h^{K/k}(R)\otimes_{K}L=h^{L/l}(R\otimes_{k}l)
. Also by associativity of the tensor product, we immediately get
(h^{\mathcal{O}_{K}/k}(R)\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}\mathcal{O}_{L})\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{L}}l=h^{\mathcal{O}_{L}/l}(R\otimes_{k}l)\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{L}}l

and
h^{\mathcal{O}_{K}/k}(R)\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}\mathcal{O}_{L}
is
\mathcal{O}_{L}
-flat by base change of the
\mathcal{O}_{K}
-flat algebra
h^{\mathcal{O}_{K}/k}(R)
; it is
\mathcal{O}_{l}
-étale if
R
is étale. To see it is
\mathfrak{m}_{L}
-adically complete, remark that
\mathfrak{m}_{L}^{e}\mathcal{O}_{L}\subseteq\mathfrak{m}_{K}\mathcal{O}_{L}\subseteq\mathfrak{m}_{L}\mathcal{O}_{L}
where
e
is the ramification index of
L/K
, so by
[
34
]
(Lem. 0319), the notions of
\mathfrak{m}_{K}\mathcal{O}_{L}
- and
\mathfrak{m}_{L}\mathcal{O}_{L}
-completeness for
\mathcal{O}_{L}
-modules coincide. Then we have :

\displaystyle\varprojlim_{n}[(h^{\mathcal{O}_{K}/k}(R)\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}\mathcal{O}_{L})/\mathfrak{m}_{K}^{n}\mathcal{O}_{L}]

\displaystyle=\varprojlim_{n}[(h^{\mathcal{O}_{K}/k}(R)/\mathfrak{m}_{K}^{n}\mathcal{O}_{K})\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}\mathcal{O}_{L}]

\displaystyle=\varprojlim_{n}[(h^{\mathcal{O}_{K}/k}(R)/\mathfrak{m}_{K}^{n}\mathcal{O}_{K})]\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}\mathcal{O}_{L}

\displaystyle=h^{K/k}(R)\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}\mathcal{O}_{L}

where the second equality holds because
\mathcal{O}_{L}
is a finite and flat, thus finite free by
[
34
]
(Lem. 02KB),
\mathcal{O}_{K}
-module. This proves
h^{\mathcal{O}_{K}/k}(R)\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}\mathcal{O}_{L}
is an
\mathfrak{m}_{L}
-complete, flat (and étale if
R/k
is étale)
\mathcal{O}_{L}
-algebra whose tensor to
l
is
R\otimes_{k}l
, thus by Proposition
89
we have
h^{\mathcal{O}_{K}/k}(R)\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{K}}\mathcal{O}_{L}=h^{\mathcal{O}_{L}/l}(R\otimes_{k}l)
.
∎
